 | `/undo` | slash | implemented | Upstream's row: `/undo`, "undo the latest tracked file operation", Session, no argument (`commands.zig:481`); `/undo now` is an unknown command in the TUI. It takes back the newest change this conversation's own `write_file` or `edit_file` committed -- the file put back as it was, or deleted when the change created it -- and says so in one neutral notice, upstream's words with the absolute path spelled terminal-safe (`\x1b`, `\x0a`, a byte that is not UTF-8 as `\xNN`): `* undo: Restored <path>`, `* undo: Deleted <path> (was newly created)`, `* undo: Could not undo <path>`, `* undo: Nothing to undo.` (`commandUndoLast`, `app_commands.zig:1242-1290`). The line shell prints the same sentence as `[shell] undo: …`. **What is tracked** is upstream's (`ChangeTracker`, `change_tracker.zig:30-99`; `publishCommittedTracker`, `app_callbacks.zig:1125-1170`): a write or an edit that landed, newest last, at most 100 deep with the oldest dropped first -- not `create_folder`, not a command, not a change that was refused, failed or changed nothing -- and a change is taken off whether or not it could be reversed. xfx also drops the oldest past 64 MiB of preimage (upstream has no byte bound; a preimage may be a 10 MiB file), always keeping the newest. **In memory only**, as upstream's: nothing is written to the session log and the model is not told; `/new`, `/clear`, `/reset` and `/resume` leave every earlier change behind (`app_session_runtime.zig:1922`, `:1999`; upstream's resume test is answered `Nothing to undo.`, `tui-resume.test.ts:6029-6037`); a provider switch does not, because it keeps the conversation, as upstream's `/provider` keeps its session and its tracker (`runProviderCommand`, `app_auth_runtime.zig:387`). **No question is asked in any permission mode** -- the person typed it, and upstream's handler calls `undoLast` directly -- **but every structural rule of a write holds**: the target is located again from the conversation's scope (an authorized root, no `..`, nothing under `.git` or `.xfx`, no symbolic link on the way), read without following a link, and replaced by a staged file renamed into its own directory with its permission bits kept, or unlinked there; a directory on the path that has gone is not made again. **Stricter than upstream** in one rule, the read-proof rule every write here obeys: the file must still be what the change left there or what this session last read of it, so a file edited by someone else since, or a link standing where it was, is `Could not undo` and left as it is (upstream restores over whatever is there, `change_tracker.zig:53-81`, `:501-537`). A read proof is left alone, so the model's next edit of a file it has not read as it now is is refused until it reads it again. **In the TUI** it is done on the runtime thread: at once between turns; typed while a turn runs it is done the moment that turn is over (upstream's is done at once) **and takes back the change that was newest when it was typed** -- the band reads the journal's position at the keystroke, and a change the turn makes after it stays for the next `/undo`; if that later change touched the same file, the undo is `Could not undo` and the file is left as the later change made it. One typed after a `/new` the runtime has not reached yet is answered for the fresh conversation. Its answer is never dropped by a reset's drain. |

Values a person typed are still never said back by the verbs (`typed`), and a profile's are never shown by `list` or `show`: neither is an approval. A server's command line and address -- in `show`, on the menu's screen, and in `approve`'s sentence -- are its file's, `${VAR}` and all, a profile server's and an approved project server's alike: what an expansion puts in is for the server (the profile and project rows; `ServerConfig::shown_command`, `shown_url`), and reaches a screen only if the server prints it to its stderr (the client runtime row). The cost: fx's goes on four columns in, so a wrapped command line or address here is led by `↳ ` where fx's is not. (2) It takes the rows it needs past fx's thirteen, up to the space the screen gives a panel, and Enter approves only what a frame drew whole -- every row in the panel's rows and none cut with `…`, as the row measures it, a cluster at a time: one that could not, or that something wrote over since (a refused write, a resize, a screen the band is blind on), approves nothing and says `xfx couldn't show all of what X runs on this screen, so nothing was approved.`. (3) Enter approves the entry that frame drew, by its digest (`TrustAction::ApproveShown`), so what is saved is the entry xfx read and showed, and a reload admits it only while the file still holds it -- a server a reload keeps keeps its screen's entry only while that entry's digest is the fresh read's, so an entry respelled (`"type": "stdio"` added) is shown, and approved, as it now is: a rewrite read before Enter approves nothing (`X changed in .mcp.json since xfx showed it; nothing was approved.`, and the screen shows the new one next), and one on disk not yet read is saved as the entry shown, which the next read finds changed and leaves waiting. fx's Approve runs `approve NAME`, which approves what its host holds when the verb runs (`verbs.zig:631-646`). An approval is for that entry, not for its name. Upstream keys it by name alone (`resolveAdmission`, `src/core/mcp/project_config.zig:825-832@c1db9191`, unchanged at `:938-945@481104fd`) while its auto mode writes `.mcp.json` without review like any other workspace file (`src/core/tooling/tool_admission.zig:663-693@c1db9191`, `:662-692@481104fd`): a model's file write could swap an approved server's command or address and have it run on the next reload, unasked. In xfx a changed entry is pending again (MCP project servers and trust, below) and waits for the menu or `approve`. An approval is for the entry, not for the workspace code it loads: `node server.js` is approved as that command line, and an edit to `server.js` asks nothing (a later unit looks at that). Three rules fx's has not, so that no answer is given to a question nobody saw (opus-C, gpt6-C): Enter answers Remove or Reject only once a frame has drawn the question, and what a refused write, a resize or a screen the band is blind on leaves unsure is unsaid again (Approve's rule, below), so a burst of Enters in one read never answers it; an Enter within 500 ms of the last -- whichever surface took it while the menu was up, a C-j that answered the model's question as Enter (QUESTION-CTRL-J), and a swallowed one too, since timing cannot tell a slow key repeat from a second tap (`MCP_ENTER_HELD`) -- answers no question, and the first swallow says `That Enter came too soon after the last one to answer the question: wait a moment, then press Enter.`; opening the full transcript or the model's question panel over the menu unsays what it drew; and while the full transcript (Ctrl-O) covers the menu, the menu takes no key. A choice runs the verb warnings-only (`.warnings_only`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:545-560`): the row's status follows, and only a warning reaches the transcript. Remove removes the name and the file its question named, both kept as it asked (`Job::Remove`, `command::remove_chosen`; no words parsed again, so a server named `--help` is removed, not answered with the overview), where fx runs `remove NAME`, which refuses a name both files hold after the question said which file; a read that finds the name in the other file before the answer cancels the question; Reject is the trust choice `reject` sends. **Gated, not offered**: fx's `Log in` and `Log out` choices sign in to a remote server, which is not built (below), so neither is a choice, and a server that needs a login says `Interactive MCP authentication is unavailable here.` on its screen where fx says `Its tools show here once you log in.`; its status is MCP-V's `needs login`. c1db9191's tabs, catalogs (Tools, Resources, Prompts), info screen, `v`, `r`, `p`, `z`, `a`, `s` and `d`/`x` keys went with its menu. `p5-mcp-menu-new` (40x120 and 40x60) and `p5-mcp-empty` against fx v0.0.16 (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/mcp-menu/`). **The verbs are fx v0.0.16's** (`src/core/mcp_host/verbs.zig@481104fd`, `src/mcp/command.rs`), in its words with `xfx` for `fx` and `~/.xfx/mcp.json` for `~/.fx/mcp.json`: the line split as fx's lexer splits it (`command_lex.tokenize_argv`, `src/core/shell_command/command_lex.zig:140-226@481104fd` -- quotes, backslashes, an unquoted operator a word of its own), and one it refuses -- a quote never closed, a backslash at the end -- `/mcp: that command line has an unmatched quote or a character xfx doesn't take here.` (`app_mcp_runtime.zig:525-531`); `help`, `-h` and `--help` the overview, `VERB -h` the verb's usage, a mistake its sentence and the usage, an unknown word `'X' isn't a /mcp command.` and the overview; `auth`, `trust`, `path`, `reload`, `resource`, `prompt` and `list --connect` refused with where each went (`verbs.zig:64-73`, `:105`); `show NAME` its `status`, `version`, `transport`, `url` or `command` (fx's quoted command line, `commandLine`, `src/core/mcp_host/config.zig:67-83@481104fd`), `source`, `error`, `next` and `tools N` rows, after waiting while a try is under way -- for at most the server's start timeout and 60 s, since one worker answers every `/mcp`; `add NAME URL` and `add NAME -- CMD [ARGS...]`, with `--env K=V`, `--header K=V` and `--project`, the name checked before anything else, written through `config::update` (MCP profile row) and said as `Added X to FILE.`, with `X is also in FILE; Every answer is under `mcp`, usage included: a verb's output one neutral notice and its errors one warning (`VerbJob.run`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:585-602`), and a change reloads in place and says nothing (`MCP client runtime` row). `p5-mcp-empty` and `p5-mcp-verbs` against fx v0.0.16 (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/mcp-verbs/`). **In typed order, where fx's is the order of completion**: fx runs each typed `/mcp` on a detached thread and reports it when it ends (`startVerbs`, `VerbJob`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:551-604`), so in `p5-mcp-verbs` its `show remote`, waiting on a connect, was printed after the `show proj` and `login fixture` typed after it; that is a race no receipt can pin, and xfx answers on the MCP runtime's one worker thread in the order typed -- so a `show` that waits holds the lines typed after it, and the menu's reads, for that wait. A word, a flag or a URL typed is said back spelled by `terminal_safe`, where fx says it as typed, and only as far as its first `=` (`/mcp add has no --env= flag.`, where fx says `--env=TOKEN=…` whole) -- a server's name too, which a hand-written profile can give one; a `--env` or `--header` value is never said back. **Refused before the write, where fx writes it**: `add --project --header Authorization=…` is `Couldn't add X to .mcp.json: xfx won't send an Authorization header from .mcp.json; add the server without --project to keep it in ~/.xfx/mcp.json.` -- xfx's reader skips such an entry (MCP project servers row), so the shared file would hold a token xfx never sends -- matched ignoring case, as the reader matches it. **And nothing the reader would skip**: the entry `add` would write is put to the reader's own rules first (`config::unreadable`, `parse_entry` over the writer's JSON), so a header name with a blank in it (` Authorization=…` included), a reserved one, a control in a value, or a URL the reader won't connect to (plain `http` off loopback, credentials, a fragment) is `Couldn't add X to FILE: its "headers" isn't valid.` / `its "url" isn't valid.` with nothing written, where fx writes it and says `Added`; a `${…}` in a project's URL is left for the expansion an approval brings, after what no expansion changes is checked (its syntax, the scheme, and no literal `@` or `#` anywhere after `://`, since where the authority ends is the expansion's to say -- **residual**: an `@` in a templated path is refused too, where fx writes it), and a profile's URL, which nothing expands, is checked as written. A choice `approve` or `reject` can't save says xfx's `Project MCP choices were not applied: …`, where fx says `Couldn't save that choice: …`. **`/mcp ` completes its argument** as fx's does (`verb_completions`, `buildCompletion`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:459-515`; `src/tui/mcp_args.rs`): the eight verbs with what each does, then `show` and `remove NAME` for each server whose name a command can carry, `login` and `logout` for a remote one, `approve` and `reject` for a project one, its status beside it, read twice a second; under the argument (from the start of the caret's row once the caret has left the argument's, the `/provider` column's anchor), no header, six rows, the hint row left to the status line (`p5-mcp-verbs` `complete-args`, `complete-show`). Up, Down, `C-j` and `C-k` move the mark, PgUp and PgDn a page of the composer's row cap, from past either end to the other end (fx's slash list, `navigateSlashCompletion(picker_delta)`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:255-260@481104fd`; `fx-p5-page-keys-mcp-24x120-args-pgdn`, `-pgup`) -- the arrows and the pages only while the composer is one row, the caret's on a taller one (`-wrapped-up`, `-wrapped-pgup`) -- Tab writes the marked line, Escape closes it for the line, and **Enter submits the marked line in place of what was typed** (`resolveSlashSubmission`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:1819-1824@481104fd`), so `/mcp remove web` with only `web2` configured removes `web2`, as fx does in `p5-mcp-verbs`. Nothing starts one before it is approved -- not the launch, a reload, or opening the menu, whose tool list for a held server is fx's reason for holding it (`heldReason`, `src/core/mcp_host/runtime.zig:2038-2053@481104fd`), never a start. **The approval's screen** is fx's (`proj  waiting for approval`, `Approve X for this project? It runs:` or `It connects to:`, then what it runs or reaches wrapped at spaces and never cut, `enter confirm     esc cancel`; presentation `:437-499`, `:507-515`), spelled before it is wrapped, so a control spelled out wraps as the cells it takes. **Where xfx's differs -- deliberately, because upstream's is unsafe to copy**: (1) it shows what the child is given, from the entry the runtime read (`ShownEntry`: its rows and the digest an approval binds, from one read) -- the command line, each word as a POSIX shell would read it back -- a word xfx expands (`${NAME}`, `${NAME:-default}`) in double quotes, every other `$`, `"`, `\` and `` ` `` escaped, so the reader expands what xfx will, and a control character beside an expansion an ANSI-C piece between quoted ones (`"a"$'\u001B'"${X}"`; one inside a default, which has no exact spelling there, stays in the quotes as the row spells it, `\x1b`) -- or the address; then one row `env: NAME=value` for each variable a stdio entry sets, or `header: Name: value` for each header a remote entry sends (a `header_env` header as `X-Team: ${TEAM}`, `bearer_token_env` as `Authorization: Bearer ${TOKEN}`, as they are sent) -- every value as written, since a pending entry is never expanded: a literal, a default with its text (`${NOPE:-./y}`), a `${VAR}` as the name it is, and never a value from xfx's environment. fx's shows the command line or the address alone (`confirmLines`, presentation `:442-481`). xfx's rule since 2026-11-04 (`c4d252f5`): a variable changes what runs as much as the command does -- `"PATH": "./x:${PATH}"` makes `node` run `./x/node`, since the command is resolved with the child's PATH, and `NODE_OPTIONS`, `LD_PRELOAD`, `DYLD_*` or `PYTHONPATH` change it as much -- so a key alone hides what is approved. The entry is the repository's own file, so its text shows nothing the file does not; a credential written into it literally (by a person, or by `/mcp add --project --env`) is shown as the file shows it, and one in xfx's environment never is. Values a person typed are still never said back by the verbs (`typed`), and a profile's are never shown by `list` or `show`: neither is an approval. A server's command line and address -- in `show`, on the menu's screen, and in `approve`'s sentence -- are its file's, `${VAR}` and all, a profile server's and an approved project server's alike: what an expansion puts in is for the server (the profile and project rows; `ServerConfig::shown_command`, `shown_url`), and reaches a screen only if the server prints it to its stderr (the client runtime row). Every row is spelled as the approval view spells a command it asks about (`approval_view::command_text`: every control `\xNN`, a line break included, and each bidirectional, zero-width or invisible code point the table names `\u{NNNN}`, so a `U+202E` cannot draw the command backwards); a row longer than 1024 bytes, which would fill the screen, is its first 512 and last 256 bytes with its length and where it is whole between them (`[N bytes in all; .mcp.json has every byte]`), never cut without saying so -- and such an entry is not shown whole, so Enter approves nothing (`A row of X's entry is too long to show here whole, so nothing was approved: .mcp.json has every byte, and /mcp approve X approves the entry as xfx read it.`). A row the width wraps goes on in rows led by `↳ `, two columns in, where each row of the entry's own is four in -- every wrapped piece, of a command line or address alone too, so none passes for a row the entry has (`v`, a run of spaces, `env: PATH=/usr/bin`; an address `https://a.example/mcp?q=`, spaces, `header: X-Team: blue`, opus-B). The cost: fx's goes on four columns in, so a wrapped command line or address here is led by `↳ ` where fx's is not. (2) It takes the rows it needs past fx's thirteen, up to the space the screen gives a panel, and Enter approves only what a frame drew whole -- every row in the panel's rows and none cut with `…`, as the row measures it, a cluster at a time: one that could not, or that something wrote over since (a refused write, a resize, a screen the band is blind on), approves nothing and says `xfx couldn't show all of what X runs on this screen, so nothing was approved.`. (3) Enter approves the entry that frame drew, by its digest (`TrustAction::ApproveShown`), so what is saved is the entry xfx read and showed, and a reload admits it only while the file still holds it -- a server a reload keeps keeps its screen's entry only while that entry's digest is the fresh read's, so an entry respelled (`"type": "stdio"` added) is shown, and approved, as it now is: a rewrite read before Enter approves nothing (`X changed in .mcp.json since xfx showed it; nothing was approved.`, and the screen shows the new one next), and one on disk not yet read is saved as the entry shown, which the next read finds changed and leaves waiting. fx's Approve runs `approve NAME`, which approves what its host holds when the verb runs (`verbs.zig:631-646`). An approval is for that entry, not for its name. Upstream keys it by name alone (`resolveAdmission`, `src/core/mcp/project_config.zig:825-832@c1db9191`, unchanged at `:938-945@481104fd`) while its auto mode writes `.mcp.json` without review like any other workspace file (`src/core/tooling/tool_admission.zig:663-693@c1db9191`, `:662-692@481104fd`): a model's file write could swap an approved server's command or address and have it run on the next reload, unasked. In xfx a changed entry is pending again (MCP project servers and trust, below) and waits for the menu or `approve`. Nothing reloads the MCP configuration without the person -- launch, and an `add`, `remove`, `approve` or `reject`, typed or chosen in the menu, are the only triggers; there is no file watch and no model tool that reloads. The typed `approve --all` keeps fx's behaviour: every entry the last read showed as a pending server, not ones added later (`CHANGELOG.md:165@481104fd`), and not one that parses only once expanded (`"type": "${T:-stdio}"` is shown as a configuration error, not as a server); `approve NAME`, typed, is the one way to approve such an entry: it has no server to find by name, so `approve` approves the entry the runtime last read, bound to that read's digest as the parsed path is, and only while `.mcp.json` still holds it (`X changed in .mcp.json since xfx read it; nothing was approved.` otherwise, and fx's `No project server named …` for an entry the runtime never read), saying what it runs or reaches but never its `env` or `headers` -- fx's `approve` finds only servers that parse (`verbs.zig:631-646@481104fd`), so this is xfx's, kept from c1db9191's `trust approve <name>`; `reject NAME` reaches such an entry the same way. An approval is for the entry, not for the workspace code it loads: `node server.js` is approved as that command line, and an edit to `server.js` asks nothing (a later unit looks at that). What a server or a repository supplied -- the menu's names, addresses, command lines, errors, stderr tails, tool names and descriptions -- is spelled as fx's footer spells it (`encodeTerminalSafeInline(.., 4096)`, `row_text.zig:127-134@481104fd`, through `terminal_safe`: controls, C1, zero-width, bidirectional and byte-order-mark code points, and the other default-ignorable ones the approval view names (SEC-A4), spelled out rather than dropped) and cut to its row, where fx writes such a byte as `?` or spells it; an approval's rows are spelled as the approval view spells a command (above), and cut only as above. A prompt with a token is not steered into a running turn, as upstream's is not (`sameTurnSteeringEligible`). **`path`**: `* skills: xfx workspace roots are auto-discovered from .xfx/skills and skills/.` and `xfx managed install root: <home>/.xfx/skills` -- upstream's third line, about compatibility roots, is not said, xfx reading none. **`create <name>`** writes upstream's template `SKILL.md` into the managed root (`* skills: Created <path>`), a name with `/` or `\` refused in upstream's words. **`remove <name>`** deletes only a managed skill (`* skills: Removed skill 'made'.`); any other is refused naming where it is (`Skill 'demo' comes from workspace .xfx/skills, not the xfx managed install root. Remove it from <path>.`). **`add`/`install <source> [--skill <name>]`** read upstream's pasted forms (`npx skills add …`, `skills.sh/<owner>/<repo>/<skill>`, `<owner>/<repo>@<skill>`) and install **from a local directory**: `* skills: Installing from <source>...`, then `* skills: Installed: <name>` per skill -- a top-level `SKILL.md` under the directory's name, each other one under its own directory's, copied without `.git*` or links into the managed root through a staged directory and two renames under `~/.xfx/.skill-install-locks/<name>` (`copySkillDirWithOptions`). **The second decided default:** a source that is not an existing directory is answered as upstream answers a clone that failed -- `* skills: Failed to install. Check the source path or URL and try again.` -- and nothing reaches the network; upstream would run `git clone --depth 1 https://github.com/<source>.git` (`installFromGitHub`, `skills.zig:265-280`). Anything else is `* usage: /skills [list|add|install|show|create|remove|path] [name|url|path]`. **The line-oriented shell** answers the same forms as `[shell] skills: …` lines, the usage line on stderr; bare, `list` and `show` print upstream's listing (`Visible skills (N):`, grouped, `listSkillsSummary`, `skill_runtime.zig:2964-3004`). **Where a link may go** (`canonicalPathHasReadAuthority`, `skill_runtime.zig:586-681`): a link in a workspace root -- the root itself, a candidate, its `SKILL.md` -- is followed only when it resolves inside the workspace ancestor the root hangs off, or inside a directory the user named: the profile's `skill_symlink_authorities` (an array of at most 32 absolute paths with no `..`, profile-only -- a `.xfx.json` that carries it is reported and ignored -- the workspace's own entry replacing the global list, each canonical where it resolves and as written where it does not yet; `config_runtime.zig:1784-1877`) or `XFX_SKILL_SYMLINK_AUTHORITIES` (colon-separated absolute paths, as written, a relative or `..` entry skipped). By default none is named, so a link out of the workspace is refused with fx's diagnostic, and a link in the managed root is never followed. **Fail closed:** an unreadable list in any profile layer names no authority at all (`invalid_value key=skill_symlink_authorities skill_symlink_authorities must be an array of at most 32 absolute directory paths without .. components`), where upstream drops only the layer it could not parse. The authorities are read once, when the configuration is loaded at launch. **Narrower than upstream's**: a candidate's root is found missing through linked directories, where upstream's walk stops at a link. Escape and Ctrl-C both stop the turn the batch belongs to, as fx's `cancelQuestionPrompt` does (`input_question_runtime.zig:59-81@481104fd`; `tui/shell.rs:9718-9727`) -- except that Escape at a freeform draft with text in it first arms a clear, on both sides (`app_input_runtime.zig:2690-2710@481104fd`; `tui/shell.rs:9786-9797`): Escape stops the turn only at an empty field. When a key stops it, the document keeps `▪ Cancelled`, the call answers `aborted by user` (`tools::question::ABORTED_OUTPUT`), which the model reads in the next prompt's request, and the sentinel never reaches the wire (`23b-question-cancelled`, `scripts/smoke-tui.sh:10483-10498`, `:10535-10545`) -- proved against a completion that asks **and then writes a file**, where the write runs when the question is answered and never happens when the turn is stopped. Seven **local adaptations** of the pinned upstream contract (`ask_user_question.zig`, `text_utils.zig`, `question_answer.zig`, `question_prompt.zig`) rather than verified differences, because on (4) and (5) only xfx's half has been read: (1) `permission_request_id` is not advertised; the approval-screen route is a separate P3-APPROVAL row. (2) The line-oriented shell returns the availability sentinel, because fx's interactive shell is xfx's *TUI*, not xfx's line shell. (3) Question, label, description and answer text are terminal-safe encoded and then size-bounded with a refusal rather than truncated, using the `max_output_bytes` reason when the encoded document itself would not fit the caller's limit. (4) Unicode in the synthetic `Other` freeform slot is implemented and observed: the shell decoder delivers `Input::Text(char)`, one insertion per (possibly multi-byte) character, and `23-question-panel` types `다시 묻지 마 — später` into the slot on a real terminal, checks the caret's column against `unicode-width`'s measurement of it, and finds that string whole in the answer document on the wire. Upstream's prompt inserts single bytes (`question_prompt.zig:322`) and its paste or IME route into that prompt is untraced here, so what is unverified is the **comparison**, not xfx's behaviour. (5) An ordinal for a choice the window is not showing is refused: xfx windows the options on a short screen and declines a number the user cannot see (`src/tui/question.rs`'s `an_ordinal_for_a_choice_the_window_is_not_showing_is_refused`), where upstream's `selectOrdinal` bounds only against the option count (`question_prompt.zig:389`) because its prompt has no window. (6) The description past its opening count is the `580a0c5d` text with the permission-screen sentence dropped (see (1)); fx v0.0.16's also names safety-review escalation, GitHub handles and gh/auth/tool blockers (`builtins/tools.zig:119-122@481104fd`). (7) A refused batch reaches the model as a failed call, `{"type":"error-text",…}`, where fx's argument refusals are a successful call's text (`executeWithRequester`, `ask_user_question.zig:117-122`, `call` `:114@481104fd`): `fx-p5-input-question21-40x120-bodies/003-completion.json` against xfx's, the same words. **A resume replays each batch as fx v0.0.16 does** (QUESTION-RESUME, `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/question-resume/`): a successful call whose saved document decodes is its resolutions, the `  N) ` rows the panel left, composed at the screen's width (`writeAnsweredQuestionResult`, `app_session_runtime.zig:4589-4607`; `composeResolvedQuestionAnswers`, `main.zig:2335-2346@481104fd`); any other result -- a refusal, a declined batch, a document that does not decode -- is the generic completed row `● Asked`, the marker in the system notice grey (`:4210-4335`; `lifecycleTerminalLine`, `transcript/runtime.zig:613-671@481104fd`), never one of a group's rows; a failed call is drawn so too, which is xfx's own mapping (fx draws a failed call's marker red, `:616`), because xfx's failed asks are its refusals (7), which fx records as successes; so a step of read, ask, read replays as two groups of one with the ask's row between them, each call written just before its result (`writeExecutionHistoryToSink`, `:4120-4127`; `statusNamesAsk`, `tool_group_projection.zig:1356-1359`, `:1372@481104fd`); and a batch the stop cut off has no result to replay, so its turn's `▪ Cancelled · What can xfx do differently?` stands alone. xfx's refusal is a failed call (7) and still replays as fx's refusal does, `● Asked`. The decode is fx's bound (`decodeJson`, `question_answer.zig:43-74@481104fd`; `tools::question::decode_answers`, read by hand): one JSON array of 1 to 20 objects -- an array where an object should be is refused (`:63`), a twenty-first element at its first byte, its value unread -- each a string `question` and a string `answer`, a key given twice in that object refused, other keys skipped at any depth, anything else not an answered batch and never a panic. It is looser than fx in one place: a skipped value is not checked, where fx's `std.json` refuses a key given twice in any object and an unpaired surrogate in any string (`duplicate_field_behavior = .@"error"`, `std/json/static.zig:20-26`, `dynamic.zig:149-156`; `Scanner.zig:25-32`; the same lines in Zig 0.16.0, 0.17.0 and 0.17.1, the releases the pin builds with, `build.zig.zon:28@481104fd`; `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/question-resume/zig-017.txt`), so `[{"question":"Q","answer":"A","x":{"k":1,"k":2}}]` and the same with `"x":"\ud800"` are answered batches here and, by those lines, null there; only a hand-forged log holds them, the rows are drawn inert, and a test pins both (`a_skipped_value_is_not_checked_as_upstreams_parse_checks_it`); the decoded texts cross `bridge::inert_hydration` and the rows' own `safe_rows`/`answer_safe` as the panel's entries do. `p5-question-resume` (batches of 1 and 4 answered, 21 and none refused, a read-ask-read step, a cancelled batch) and `p5-input-question20`'s `resumed` at 40x120, 24x80 and 14x72 match fx v0.0.16. A saved document fx would read differently is not captured: the fxcap harness has no step that rewrites a saved session between a run and its resume (`rehomefile` writes a fixed file), and no forged session was tried outside it. So the decode's rejects are pinned by tests on both sides' rules (`question_answer.zig:101-141@481104fd`; `decode_answers`'s tests), and fx's reading of the two looser documents rests on its std's source, read, not on a run. **Ctrl-O after a resume does not yet show a replayed ask's detail**: fx v0.0.16 hands each replayed call its saved arguments and result (`attachHistoricalToolDetail`, `app_session_runtime.zig:4337`, `:4607@481104fd`), so its full transcript shows `│  questions: [...]` and the saved result under the resolutions, and under `● Asked` with a `Tool` header; xfx's shows the two rows alone (`p5-question-resume-ctrlo`, ledger cause `question-resume-ctrlo-detail`). Owner unit: **QUESTION-RESUME-CTRLO** (queued). **One Left spelling goes back a question in xfx where fx leaves a choice alone**: `ESC ESC [ D`, which xfx reads as Escape and Left -- so the Escape cancels the batch -- where fx reads a meta-prefixed word move (`escape_parser.zig:83-89@481104fd`; xfx's decoder has no `meta_prefixed`, `kitty_key`'s doc in `src/tui/input.rs`), a spelling xfx's decoder folds into a key fx keeps apart, not the panel's; `probe/` in the question-word-left receipt shows fx on page 4 after it. Owner unit: **META-PREFIX-DECODE** (proposed), since xfx's decoder has no meta prefix on any surface (`docs/parity.md`'s full-screen TUI row). Shift-Left and Ctrl-B went back a question too until MODIFIED-ARROWS-SCREENS, which gives the question the event as the decoder spelled it (above), and so closes what QUESTION-LEFT-SPELLINGS (proposed) was for: fx's Shift-Left is a composer move (`escape_parser.zig:99-106@481104fd`) and its Ctrl-B no question action at a choice (`questionActionFromByte`, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:135-153`, `:199-209`; `app_input_runtime.zig:1136-1160@481104fd`). `tools.zig:1374`. |

 | tool permission modes | tool group | implemented | `ask` requires a real TTY approval, discloses a bounded excerpt of the change, states what "always" would grant, and denies when there is no approval channel; `auto` admits bounded workspace writes and a reporting-only command grammar that cannot compile or run project code; `yolo` skips policy and prints a warning to stderr. -<<<<<<< 88e83a68 (Merge P5 train 15, 4 of 5: the approval stack (fix/p5-rule-confirm-keys @ 7c9dfb2e))
-| full-screen TUI | ui | partial | Opt-in and narrower than upstream's. `XFX_TUI=1` on a bare `xfx` takes the terminal into raw mode on its **normal** buffer: the band, the transcript and the composer live there for the life of the session and never move, and mouse reporting is never enabled, so the terminal's own scrollback and wheel keep working. The alternate screen is an owned excursion and nothing else -- `\x1b[?1049h` is written only to review a change too big for the band to show, for as long as that question is unanswered (below), and for the full transcript `C-o` opens, for as long as it is open (its own row below); a session that does neither, however long, writes it never. Under tmux the kitty keyboard push is omitted, because sending it there breaks key input. Raw mode is an obligation rather than a feature: the entry bits (`ECHO`/`ICANON`/`IEXTEN`/`ISIG` clear, `CS8`, `VMIN=1`, `VTIME=0`) are asserted positively while the child runs, and the `termios` captured before entry is put back on exit -- onto the descriptor it was captured from, and whether or not the screen could still be written. The band is a divider, the composer, and a hint row at the bottom of the screen -- with one more row above the divider while a turn is running, saying what it is doing, and a block of rows between the two while that turn is waiting to be told whether it may change something; every frame is one write wrapped in synchronized output (`?2026h`/`l`) -- a frame that also grows the document carries those rows inside the same update, ahead of the band, as upstream's frame carries its movement bytes (`composeWireFrame`, `src/ui/render_engine/terminal_diff.zig:604-645` at `c1db919`), so a terminal that honours the mode never presents a new row with the band still where the scroll left it -- and what that write carries is the **difference** between what the terminal is holding and what it should be holding: the band keeps a shadow grid of grapheme cells, wide-cell continuations and attribute state, builds the grid the frame would leave, and emits a `CUP`, replacement text and an `EL` for the rows that changed and for nothing else -- so a keystroke costs the cell it changed and an animation phase costs the digit it turned over. **A frame whose cells, window title and caret are all unchanged writes zero bytes**, which is what keeps an idle session silent between the loop's 8 ms ticks. The shadow is advanced only by a write that succeeded, so a refused frame is owed again rather than believed; a Ctrl-L and the resume after a `SIGTSTP` mark the screen externally damaged, and the frame after either opens with the Phase-1 erase -- the rows a shrinking band gave back, then `CUP` to the band's top row and `ED` -- because a forgotten shadow is *unknown* rather than blank, and a diff that treated it as blank would rewrite the band's own columns and leave whatever the shell put beside them on every band row. The erase starts at the band's top row and never above it, so the terminal's own document survives a resume. A resume also re-arms the window title: the restore that hands the terminal back pops the title stack, so the title the session set is gone, and the band owes a fresh `OSC 2` -- one push per time the terminal is taken and one pop per time it is given back, so a stop/resume cycle leaves the stack balanced. The optimization is judged by the screen it leaves rather than by the bytes it saves: the `fault-injection` build keeps the Phase-1 whole-band painter behind a compile-time seam (`Fault::FullPaintReference`, absent from every released binary), and `scripts/smoke-tui.sh`'s scenario 13 drives identical facts through both painters on a real pseudoterminal and requires the final cells, attributes and caret to be equal. **The terminal's window title is set** -- `OSC 2 ; xfx · <model>`, with every control stripped out of the model label so a configured name cannot close the sequence and have the rest of itself executed -- and it is borrowed rather than taken: the mode set pushes the terminal's own title onto its stack (`CSI 22 ; 2 t`) and every restore path, normal and abnormal, pops it back (`CSI 23 ; 2 t`). A title is written as its own `OSC 2` right ahead of the frame's synchronized update and never inside it, which is where upstream's lands: upstream writes a title the moment it changes, as a write of its own between frames (`syncTerminalTitleWith`, `src/core/app/app_session_runtime.zig:3608-3629`; `setTerminalTitleLabel`, `src/ui/render.zig:723-733` at `c1db919`), so its bytes reach the terminal after one frame's `?2026l` and before the next one's `?2026h`; the output check refuses an update led by anything else, or by a title other than the one the band owes. The line-oriented shell sets no title and pushes nothing. **What has already reached the terminal's own scrollback is never rewritten**: the diff writes only at or below the band's top row, plus the rows a shrinking band gave back, and a document append still scrolls the screen with real linefeeds -- committed rows stay the terminal's. A terminal smaller than 6 rows or 20 columns is refused with a reason rather than painted over. **What those rows say**: the divider spans the screen, the composer shows what has been typed into it behind its prompt marker, and the hint row says what a turn would be run with -- in upstream's order, joined by upstream's separator: a call to action when the configured provider has no usable credential (a Gateway with no bearer token or a llmux with no URL -- both refuse every turn, and the row says so before a prompt is spent on one), the permission mode, and a compact model label that drops the provider prefix and the `claude-` family prefix. A refusal takes the left of it whole when one has just happened, and `esc again to clear` sits flush with the last column while the double-Esc gesture is armed, the row's colours closed before its padding as upstream's are (`input_presentation.zig:551-567`). On a screen too narrow for all of it the segments are **dropped whole, from the right**, rather than clipped mid-number: the permission mode gives way in front of the model rather than the model behind it, and the right-hand warning is reserved before the left is measured, because it is about a key that is already armed. The meter upstream ends that row with is here too and is filled from a completed turn's own `input_tokens` over the context window the provider's catalog publishes, shown only when the session has both numbers and dropped whole rather than defaulted when it has one; what is still absent is the shortcut legend. **While a turn is in flight there is one row above the divider**, and only then: it says `• Thinking` with how long the turn has been running, or names the tool that is running instead of saying `Thinking` at all -- a turn that has gone quiet because a tool is taking a minute otherwise looks exactly like a turn that has gone quiet. The marker blinks every 500 ms, counted in the renderer's own 50 ms animation phases rather than on a second clock beside it, and the row asks for a frame when its text changes rather than on every phase, so a band whose row says the same thing is not repainted twenty times a second and an idle band asks for nothing at all. The row is the **band's** while it exists: it takes its row from the bottom of the document exactly as a composer that grew by one does, and the divider, the composer and the caret do not move when a turn starts. It is **exactly the turn's lifetime**: the runtime says when a turn begins and says when it ends, in order on the one channel that carries everything else it says, and the band draws the row between the two. Told rather than counted, and that is correctness rather than taste -- a prompt is accepted the moment it is typed and may then wait behind another turn for a minute (the hint row is where waiting is said, and a waiting prompt gets no row), and the place a finished turn holds is given back *after* its conclusion is sent, so a band counting places would announce a turn that had not started and start a clock for one that no longer exists. **The clock is one turn's**: a prompt queued behind a running one does not restart it, and when that turn ends the row begins again from zero for whatever turn the runtime starts next rather than carrying the finished one's elapsed time forward. A `/model` travels on the same channel but is not a turn, and no row appears for one. Two things named rather than implied: the elapsed clock **stops while xfx is waiting for a decision**, because that interval measures the person rather than the model -- the approval panel below is what pends one, and the row itself is off the band while a question is up, as upstream's is (`render_input.zig:705`), and comes back at the elapsed time it stopped at; and the token count upstream shows beside the elapsed time is **absent**, because the streamed event set carries no usage -- the row says nothing about tokens rather than reporting a zero nobody measured. What is *above* the divider is the turn, described below. The band's size is asked of **standard output**, because that is the screen it is drawn on; raw mode is entered on standard input, because that is where input arrives; and a redirected session whose two ends are different terminals gets no cursor report at all, so it starts at row 1 and pushes nothing. A screen that refuses every frame for half a second of wall-clock time ends the session with the first of those errors, on a terminal the exit still puts back, rather than being retried invisibly forever; the budget is measured on a clock rather than in repaints, because the 8 ms tick is the longest a turn waits and not the shortest. The exit clears from the band's top row downward (`app_lifecycle.zig:578-593`), and only when a band was really painted: a session that left before its first frame -- a Ctrl-D that arrived with the cursor report -- moves no cursor and erases nothing. At launch it asks the terminal where the cursor is (CSI `6n`, 100 ms deadline, row 1 if the terminal does not answer; the row under it when the cursor is past the first column) and pushes the shell output above that row into scrollback -- or, with `/settings`' `Startup scrollback` off, starts on that row under it and pushes only what leaves fewer than eleven document rows (`app_lifecycle.zig:944-988`) -- moving the cursor to the bottom row first, because a linefeed scrolls a terminal only from the bottom margin and from anywhere else merely walks the cursor down -- so the band never paints over what was already on screen; a keystroke typed while the query is in flight is deferred, not swallowed. Restoration is proven one case per exit path, each on a real pty: the normal exit, `SIGTERM`/`SIGHUP`/an external `SIGINT` (the child dies *by the signal*, which is what proves the handler reset the disposition and re-raised rather than fabricating a status) -- **except a `SIGINT` the process was started ignoring** (`nohup … &`), which stays ignored, so a `kill -INT` changes nothing and every command the session runs inherits the ignore, as upstream's TUI catches no `SIGINT` at all (`installAbnormalExitHandlers` owns `SIGTERM` and `SIGHUP` only, `app_lifecycle.zig:82-104` at `c1db9191`) -- and `SIGTSTP`/`SIGCONT` including a **second** `SIGTSTP` after a resume, which is what proves the stop handler was reinstalled. Every one of those is an ordinary transition rather than a race, because the owned signals are blocked across the whole of it -- from before the `tcsetattr` that takes the terminal until after the last handler is installed -- so a supervisor that signals xfx during startup finds a handler rather than a default disposition and a raw terminal. The signals named here are the ones a *supervisor or an operator* sends: with `ISIG` cleared a **typed** Ctrl-C or Ctrl-Z generates no signal at all, and arrives as a byte for the input decoder. Ctrl-C is a byte, not a signal, and it means what upstream's semantic Ctrl-C means (`app_input_runtime.zig:1675-1712` at `c1db919`): over a running turn with a draft in the composer it takes the draft and nothing else; otherwise every press is a press of one three-second exit window -- the first arms it, says `press ctrl+c again to exit` on the hint row for as long as it lasts (the `/help` catalog's row included), and stops the running turn or throws the idle draft away; a second inside it leaves with **130**, disjoint from the external `SIGINT` above, which still kills the process by the signal unless the launcher ignored it; any other keystroke closes the window early. A stopped turn leaves upstream's line, `▪ Cancelled · What can xfx do differently?`, at the keystroke: the answer stops where it was on the screen (the line it had not finished is not painted, nor is anything still crossing from the runtime) and no `✗ system:` or summary follows; a running command is killed at the keystroke with its process group, its row `└ Cancelled <command>` and its line `▪ Cancelled <command> · …` the turn's (the UI raises the turn's own flag, which the command's supervisor polls), where a running file call runs to its end first -- **and the interrupt drops whatever was queued behind it**, said as `⊘ queue: dropping what was queued behind it as well.`, because a queue that started running a moment after the user asked everything to stop would be the surprise the band's `┋` row is there to prevent; a prompt typed *after* the interrupt is a new intention and still runs, which the interrupt is told the boundary of rather than left to guess. What the model is told about the stopped turn is upstream's: the cut-off answer closed with `The previous response ended before completion.` (or the tools that finished, named), a call the turn stopped under -- one that never ran, a command the user stopped, or a question the user cancelled -- answered `aborted by user` as a failure, then the `<turn_aborted>` user message (`session.zig:52-64`, `:2695-2747`); any failed tool result is sent as a failure (`error-text` on the Gateway, `is_error` on llmux; `vercel_protocol.zig:742-747`), and a refusal as `execution-denied` with the refusal as its reason (`vercel_protocol.zig:742-744`; `is_error` on llmux, which has no word of its own). A typed Ctrl-Z still binds nothing -- upstream's suspends (`app_input_runtime.zig:862-866@481104fd`), so here only a `SIGTSTP` stops the session; owner unit: **CTRL-Z** (queued) -- and Ctrl-D is not an exit while a turn is running. Escape is a two-tap: over a running turn a second one within a second stops it, and the hint row says `esc again to interrupt` (narrower: `esc esc interrupt`, `esc esc`) in between; at an idle prompt with a draft a second one within 500 ms clears the composer, and the row says `esc again to clear`, because nothing else in this phase discards a draft without a key that says so; at an empty idle composer Escape arms nothing (`app_input_runtime.zig:3210-3213`). A prompt submitted while a turn is running is upstream's steer, delivered into that turn at upstream's boundaries (`worker_runtime.zig:1080-1104`, `orchestrator.zig:157-261`): over a tool-free answer at once -- the answer cut where it had got to and kept as the assistant's message -- while a tool runs after its results, and with the model's answer after it, the turn going on; the model is told each in upstream's `<user_steering>` wrapper and a later turn replays the words as typed. It is the band's `┋ <prompt>` row in the dim grey until the turn takes it and its card is written then; Up at an empty composer takes the newest back while it still waits for a tool (`popQueuedSteerForEdit`); a stop drops steers with the queue, and one the turn never took runs as the next prompt. A request the turn retries (a status the server says is transient, a connection that never opened) replaces the activity row with upstream's recovery label, `⚠ Provider unavailable · HTTP 500 · server_error: upstream exploded · retrying request in 1s`, counting down to the next attempt and kept through a retried answer arriving, then `✓ recovered · attempt N` for 1.5 s or until the turn ends (`worker_status.zig:5`, `:221-235`), a label too wide for the screen wrapping onto up to three rows under its mark (`render_input.zig:406`, `:689-697`); the retry budget is xfx's own three attempts, not upstream's (re-verified against fx v0.0.16, `481104fd`, which changes nothing a retry shows: upstream's label, `recoveryLabel`, `src/core/shared/types.zig:539-606@481104fd`, differs from 0.0.13's only by an action the retry path never takes, and its count still rounds up, `format_retry_label`, `src/core/output/worker_status.zig:201-219`, `:212@481104fd` -- xfx's `src/tui/retry.rs:369-381`, `:384-391`, with its three causes at `:252-261`; the statuses retried are still 429, 500, 502, 503 and 504, `fromHttpStatus`, `src/core/agent/stream_provider.zig:480-492`, and `isRetryableModelFailure`, `src/core/agent/runtime/orchestrator.zig:4496-4501@481104fd`, as xfx's `src/gateway/mod.rs:1005-1006`; 0.0.16's one change on that path, `fe7925b2`, deletes a stall stop a status failure could never reach; and upstream's turn still never ends on a transient failure, backing off 250 ms, 1 s, 2 s and on to 30 s and slowing to once a minute after fifteen minutes, `src/core/agent/runtime/model_response_recovery.zig:5-9`, `:270-282@481104fd`, where xfx's three attempts wait 250 and then 500 ms, `src/gateway/mod.rs:59`, `src/agent/machine.rs:78-85`, `:200-208`), and Escape over a retry arms the interrupt because upstream pauses only its unbudgeted wait for connectivity, which xfx does not have. That decoder is the session's own: bytes in, keystrokes out, one machine fed in arrival order (the keystrokes the launch probe read with the cursor report included), with a lone `ESC` resolved as the Escape key only after 50 ms of quiet, an unfinished `CSI` waited for however long the terminal takes, an unknown sequence bounded at 32 bytes and reported as a keystroke with no binding rather than as a phantom Escape, `ESC` followed by a control byte delivered as the Escape key *and* that byte -- except `ESC DEL`, which is Alt-Backspace and deletes back over one word of word characters, as upstream's does (`composer_deletion.zig:103-136`) where `C-w` deletes back to whitespace, and `ESC CR`/`ESC LF`, which is Alt-Enter and inserts a newline (`escape_parser.zig` at `c1db919`, :515-519), because replaying either would make two of them two Escapes inside the double-Esc window and throw the whole draft away, and an Alt-Enter a submit besides -- and **no** C0 byte or control scalar ever delivered as text -- a tab included, because a control that reached the composer would reach the terminal again on submit; a tab is a *named keystroke* instead, which the approval panel walks its choices with and the composer has no binding for. **Key reports are decoded in both of their spellings** -- kitty's `CSI <key> ; <mods> u` and `modifyOtherKeys`' `CSI 27 ; <mods> ; <key> ~` -- through one port of upstream's `kittyUnicodeKeyAction` (`escape_parser.zig` at `c1db919`, :185-266), in its branch order: a Ctrl letter is that letter's control byte and therefore its binding, Escape, Enter with a modifier, Alt-Backspace, Alt-b/f, Super+Z and the keypad are the keys they already are here, Caps Lock and Num Lock are not modifiers, and a key **release** is never a keystroke; Super+A, Super+C and Super+X are the composer's selection (:223-231) -- select the whole draft, copy it, cut it, through the clipboard `/copy` uses, the copy silent and the cut taking the text out only once the clipboard has it (`cutSelection`, `input_selection_runtime.zig:32-42`), the selected run painted in reverse video (`input_presentation.zig:995-1021`); Alt-d deletes the word to the right; Ctrl+O opens the full transcript; and a page key pages whichever surface has the keys -- the full transcript while it is open, on the bare key only (`.page_up`/`.page_down`, `pageKeyAction`, `escape_parser.zig:169-173`; `keyForAction`, `input_full_transcript_runtime.zig:156-178@481104fd`): held with a modifier, in the keypad's spelling (Shift, Alt, Ctrl or Super; a lock bit alone is masked off, `escape_parser.zig:186-187`) or the tilde's `CSI 5`/`6 ; <mod> ~` (any bit, a lock bit and a bit past eight counted, `escape_parser.zig:699`), the key is upstream's composer move to a page (`escape_parser.zig:171`, `escape_parser.zig:708-709@481104fd`), which the full transcript does not take (`app_input_runtime.zig:930`), and it goes on to the composer behind the screen (`app_input_runtime.zig:997-1001@481104fd`), here as there -- except for `/mcp`'s menu, which under the full transcript takes no key at all here, xfx's recorded choice (`offer_to_mcp`, `src/tui/shell.rs`), where fx's pages its list behind the screen (`routeMcpHostMenuMove`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:396@481104fd`): the decoder keeps it apart (`ComposerPageUp`/`ComposerPageDown`, `src/tui/input.rs`) until the full transcript, the question and the approval have read it, and folds it back to the page key for every surface after them (`folded`, `src/tui/shell.rs`; `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/modified-page-keys/`) -- the list of an open menu or picker by the composer's row cap (`routeComposerVertical`'s `picker_delta`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:222-233@481104fd`; `routeHelpMenuMove`, `:465-483`), and otherwise the composer's caret by the same number of rows (`fx-p5-input-row-limit-41x120-at-pgdn`, `-help-pgdn`, `-slash-pgdn`, `-grown-pgup`), bare or held alike, as upstream's bare page key is the same composer move (`shortcuts.zig:53-54@481104fd`); the question and the approval take neither (`input_question_runtime.zig:149-200`, `input_approval_runtime.zig:90-128@481104fd`). So do the three lists that took none until unit PAGE-KEYS-SETTINGS-COLUMN: `/settings`' menu and its Model row's list (`routeSettingsMenuMove`, `:431-463@481104fd`), the `/model ` column in each of its stages and an `/effort ` line's (`navigateModelPicker`, `:1072-1096@481104fd`), and `/mcp `'s argument completions, which fx counts as a slash list (`slashCompletionCount` over the registry's `arguments`, `command_specs.zig:578-585`, `:876-889@481104fd`) and pages by `picker_delta` (`input_completion_runtime.zig:255-260`); on each a page past either end lands on the other end, not round the list (`list_window.zig:45-53`, `settings_catalog.zig:229-233@481104fd`), and `C-j`/`C-k` move a row (`routePickerControlByte`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1093-1108@481104fd`) (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/page-keys-settings-column/`, `fx-p5-page-keys-*-24x120-*`). As fx's, a slash list -- the slash menu and `/mcp `'s arguments -- takes the arrows and a page only while the composer is one row (`capped.input_extra == 0`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:250-260@481104fd`); on a taller one they move the caret and the mark stays, while `C-j`/`C-k` walk it at any height (`routeSlashCompletionMove`, `:175-180`; `fx-p5-page-keys-mcp-24x120-wrapped-*`); `/settings`, the column and the other lists fx asks first (`routeNonSlashPickerMove`, `:230`) take them at any height. A list that hangs under an argument -- the column, `/provider`'s and `/mcp `'s -- is drawn from the argument's column while the caret is on its row and from the start of the caret's row otherwise (`projectedAnchorColumn`, `input_presentation.zig:383-386`, `visual_layout.zig:705-713@481104fd`). Over an empty `/settings` list every move goes on to the composer, as fx's does when `Menu.move` answers `false` (`settings_catalog.zig:227`): Up and Down to the search's start and end (`-settings-24x120-empty-up`, `-empty-down`). While the full transcript is up, `C-j` and `C-k` reach no list -- they are the composer's newline and kill to the end -- as fx's `routePickerControlByte` refuses them there (`composerPickerSurfaceVisible`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1094`, `:1110-1116@481104fd`); the `@` list's keys there are CTRLO-AT-KEYS's (its own row). On these lists fx's Home and End are caret moves (`shortcuts.zig:51-52`, `app_input_runtime.zig:298-309@481104fd`), as xfx's are; `C-n` is `history_next` (`shortcuts.zig:17`, `app_input_runtime.zig:403-407`) and `C-p` no composer shortcut (`shortcuts.zig:107-117`), so neither walks them on either side; and the wheel does nothing at fx's composer (`app_input_runtime.zig:1036-1040`), where xfx asks for no mouse reports. `/mcp`'s menu takes a page key as one step, as fx's does (`routeMcpHostMenuMove`, `:424-429@481104fd`). What upstream resolves to a key xfx still does not have -- a selection-extending move, spelled on a letter (Ctrl+Shift+A/B/E/F, Alt+Shift+B/F) or with Shift on a page key, and the all-sessions toggle (`open_all_sessions`, :253) -- binds nothing beyond the plain move: Ctrl+Shift+A/B/E/F are the line's start, the step left, the line's end and the step right, fx's `line_start`, `character_left`, `line_end` and `character_right` with the Shift dropped (`escape_parser.zig:235-242@481104fd`; COMPOSER-SUPER-ARROWS round 2, `ctrl_shift_a_b_e_f_are_upstreams_moves_the_selection_dropped`; before it they bound nothing), and Alt+Shift+B/F are the word moves with the Shift dropped, as the arrows' is (`escape_parser.zig:244`, `:248@481104fd`; a question's draft drops it in fx too, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:155-197@481104fd`), until the composer has a selection to extend (owner unit **COMPOSER-SELECTION-EXTEND**, queued); Shift-Tab, upstream's `toggle_permission_mode` (:254), is the permission-mode cycle (the `/permissions` row). A selection-extending **arrow** is the exception and is not a dead key: kitty's Up and Down (`57352`/`57353`) and the keypad's arrows, Home and End resolve through xfx's own cursor table, which drops Shift together with the selection it would have extended, so with Shift held -- alone or beside Ctrl or Alt -- the key makes the plain move it makes here unshifted (a word move for Left and Right under Ctrl or Alt) where upstream would extend a selection (on Up and Down the move is upstream's own, the selection apart: below); the legacy `CSI 1 ; <mod> A` spelling takes the same table, and so do the tilde Home and End with a modifier (`CSI 1`/`7 ; <mod> ~`, `CSI 4`/`8 ; <mod> ~`, below). **A held Up or Down is upstream's own move, and only Ctrl's reaches the history** (COMPOSER-PARAGRAPH-MOVE): Alt-Up and -Down, with Shift or Ctrl or neither, are `paragraph_up`/`paragraph_down` (`modifiedArrowAction`, `escape_parser.zig:83-85@481104fd`), the caret to the start of its paragraph or, from there or from a blank line, of the one before, and to the next paragraph's start or the draft's end, a blank line being one of spaces, tabs and carriage returns only (`previousParagraphStart`/`nextParagraphStart`, `lineIsBlank`, `text_boundaries.zig:91-147@481104fd`, copied byte for byte); Super-Up and -Down, with Shift or without, are `draft_start`/`draft_end` (`escape_parser.zig:74-76`), the draft's start and end (`horizontal_navigation.zig:29-30`). Both are the caret move alone -- no picker walks on them, no edge jump, no steer, no history -- and both put a later `/model` column back to the models (`abandonModelPickerOptions`, `routeComposerShortcutAction`, `app_input_runtime.zig:298-309@481104fd`; `Shell::offer_to_column`). Shift- and Ctrl-Shift-Up and -Down are `visual_up`/`visual_down` extending a selection (`escape_parser.zig:90-106`): an open picker walks on them as on the arrows (`routeNonSlashPickerMove` and the slash completion, `input_completion_runtime.zig:230-259`), and the composer moves one row and stops at the draft's first and last row, forgetting the column the run aimed for there (`applyTargetWithSelection`, `vertical_navigation.zig:70-73`; `if (page or extend_selection) return;`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:270@481104fd`) -- no edge, no steer, no history. Ctrl-Up and -Down are fx's plain `visual_up`/`visual_down`, the arrows to the pickers and to the composer, edge, steer and history included (`input_completion_runtime.zig:272-291`). None of them moves a compact menu -- `/statusline`, the read-only panel, `/usage`, `/workspace` -- or a rule change's confirmation, which read the plain arrows only (`else => return`, `app_input_runtime.zig:2259-2263@481104fd`; the approval's `else => {}`, `input_approval_runtime.zig:128`). xfx's decoder reads Alt on Up and Down as `ParagraphUp`/`ParagraphDown`, Ctrl alone as `ComposerUp`/`ComposerDown`, Shift as `SelectUp`/`SelectDown` and Super as `DraftStart`/`DraftEnd` (`cursor`, `src/tui/input.rs`); the shell folds none of them (`folded`), the pickers read Ctrl's and Shift's as the arrows (`arrow_to_pickers`), and the composer each as its own move (`Shell::act`). `p5-paragraph-move` and `p5-paragraph-move-menus` show it (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/composer-paragraph-move/`): before, each recalled the line before on the draft's first row and at an empty composer, and walked a compact menu. Two differences remain: Shift's selection (COMPOSER-SELECTION-EXTEND), and a row move whose target is inside a collapsed paste, which fx refuses (`decideTarget`, `vertical_navigation.zig:20`) and xfx lands beside, as its plain arrows do. **Super with Left or Right is the line's start or end, and Ctrl or Super with Home or End the draft's, as upstream's** (COMPOSER-SUPER-ARROWS): `modifiedArrowAction` reads Super first -- Left and Right `line_start`/`line_end` whatever else is held, Home and End `draft_start`/`draft_end` -- then Alt, whose arm has no Home or End, so Home and End with Alt and without Super are the plain keys, then Ctrl (Home and End `draft_start`/`draft_end`), then Shift (Home and End `line_start`/`line_end`) (`escape_parser.zig:73-108@481104fd`); the keypad's Left, Right, Home and End take the same table (`navigationKeyAction`, `:163-167`), and the tilde Home and End are the draft's edge with Ctrl's bit in the field and the line's without it, Super or not (`:699-707`, below). The line is the one the line breaks delimit, not the screen row (`horizontal_navigation.zig:31-38`; `logicalLineStart`/`logicalLineEnd`, `text_boundaries.zig:79-89@481104fd`), and the composer moves the caret there and nowhere else (`app_input_runtime.zig:298-309`). xfx's decoder spells the line's edges `Action::LineStart`/`LineEnd` and the draft's `Action::DraftStart`/`DraftEnd`, Super-Up's (`cursor`, `src/tui/input.rs`). To upstream `.home` is `line_start` (`shortcuts.zig:51-52`; past the composer's route `.home` is `unreachable`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1025`), and no surface tells the two apart: the full transcript leaves both to the composer (`input_full_transcript_runtime.zig:156-178`), a question's field and an approval's amendment take both as their start and their choices neither (`src/ui/input/runtime.zig:82-83`, `:162-163`), the menus act on neither -- `/statusline`, `/usage` and the settings menu move on the plain Left and Right alone (`app_input_runtime.zig:984-992`, `:2228-2263`) -- and a `/model` column is dropped as Home drops it (`:298-309`). So xfx's question reads the line's edges as Home and End (`question::Act::of`) and the shell folds them to Home and End once the full transcript and the approval have read the event (`src/tui/shell.rs`'s `folded`); the draft's edges were already every reader's (above). In an approval's amendment, which xfx lets hold a line break, Home is that line's start and so is Super-Left (APPROVAL-PASTE-SPACES, below). Shift's selection is dropped (COMPOSER-SELECTION-EXTEND). `p5-super-arrows`, `p5-super-arrows-question` and `p5-super-arrows-approval` show it (`.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/composer-super-arrows/`); before COMPOSER-SUPER-ARROWS Super-Left and -Right were the one-character step, with Ctrl or Alt held too the word move, and Home and End the line's edges whatever was held. Every key herdr 0.9.3 (libghostty-vt, kitty flag 1) was captured sending in that form decodes to its binding, and its `u`-form Ctrl-U and Ctrl-D are driven end to end on a real pty (`.prd/tui-phase3/receipts/2026-10-06-herdr-csi-u-keys.md`). The grammar is stricter than upstream's -- one canonical decimal spelling per field, no extra field -- everywhere but the tilde keys, whose grammar is upstream's (below). **The tilde Home and End are upstream's** (DECODER-TILDE-HOME-END): `CSI 1 ~` and rxvt's `CSI 7 ~` are Home, `CSI 4 ~` and rxvt's `CSI 8 ~` End (`escape_parser.zig:628-635@481104fd`), and each with a modifier (`CSI 7 ; 5 ~`, any field) is upstream's move: the draft's edge with Ctrl's bit in the field and the line's without it, Super, a lock bit and a bit past eight read as any modifier (`escape_parser.zig:699-707`; COMPOSER-SUPER-ARROWS, which made the draft's edge; before it Home or End whatever was held) -- the line's edge being the bare key's move on every surface the bare key reaches: the composer, a question's field, an approval's draft and the lists, as upstream's `.home` is its composer move to the line's start (`shortcuts.zig:51-52`; `terminal_action_decoder.zig:214-224@481104fd`). **The tilde keys' grammar is upstream's** (TILDE-EMPTY-MODIFIER): every field is a number that stops at 65535 (`escape_parser.zig:19-23`, `:583-603`, `:662-666`, `:741-744@481104fd`), so a modifier field of `1`, empty or `0` holds no modifier and is the bare key (`CSI 1 ; 1 ~` is Home and `CSI 3 ; ~` Delete, `:696`, `:712-718`), a leading zero is the number (`CSI 07 ~` is Home and `CSI 3 ; 05 ~` Ctrl-Delete, `:600-602`), a modifier past 256 is its bits (`CSI 1 ; 257 ~` is Home), and **Delete reads every bit of its field** (`:694-698`, `:176-180`): Caps Lock or Num Lock alone (`CSI 3 ; 65 ~`, `; 129 ~`, `; 193 ~`) or a bit past eight alone (`CSI 3 ; 257 ~`) is nothing, and with Super among them it kills to the line's end (`CSI 3 ; 265 ~`). A third field is a key report whatever the first holds (`CSI 1 ; 5 ; 99 ~` is Ctrl-C, `:668-674`, `:746-752`), and the paste markers keep their one spelling, as upstream's do (`digit_count == 3`, `:625-626`). Left apart, older than TILDE-EMPTY-MODIFIER and the same for every `CSI`: a tilde sequence of 32 parameter bytes or more -- a field with a run of leading zeros, or digits past where upstream's number stops -- is not read here (`MOST_BYTES`, `src/tui/input.rs`): with 32 to 63 it is nothing, and with 64 or more the 64th ends the discard and every byte after it is typed (`ESC [` + 64 zeros + `3~` is nothing and then the text `3~`), where upstream's accumulators, which have no bound (`escape_parser.zig:600-603`, `:663-666`, `:741-744@481104fd`), read Delete. `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/tilde-empty-modifier/` shows each class in the composer (`p5-tilde-empty-modifier`, 15 of 15 snaps identical to fx). `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/decoder-tilde-home-end/` shows the bare and modified keys in the composer (`p5-decoder-tilde-home-end`, 9 of 9 snaps identical to fx). Bracketed paste is framed: `?2004h` is enabled, the bytes between `ESC[200~` and `ESC[201~` are content rather than keys -- so a newline inside a paste does not submit it, a `0x03` does not cancel a turn, and an escape sequence is not obeyed -- and anything over 1000 codepoints is shown in the composer as `[Pasted text #N, M lines]` (`1 line` for one) and expanded back into the prompt when it is sent -- the prompt's card, the turn's estimate and a steer's card are that expanded text, every line on rows of its own, as upstream's card is (`input_submit_runtime.zig:705-706`, `user_message_card.zig:348-383`, `orchestrator.zig:5482-5485`) -- expanded to the text the frame made of it, which is the pasted bytes filtered, decoded as UTF-8 with anything that is not UTF-8 replaced, and with CR and CRLF line breaks normalized to LF, rather than to the bytes the terminal sent. **A prompt's card is shown in the frame that clears the composer**, as fx v0.0.16's 70611a91 shows one (`submit` queues the card in the `route_bytes` that takes the draft, `src/tui/shell.rs` `send`/`echo`): whole, when it fits between the document's last row and fx's fixed content bottom, `rows - 4` (`footer_rows`, `src/main.zig:173@481104fd`; `p5-transcript-submit` wire, the first frame after Enter); otherwise only its newest rows -- as many as that room holds, at least one, and no more than the frame's band leaves under the row above the card and the blank row over the activity row -- at the band's top, and the card itself, whole, in the next frame, once that one has landed (`buildPendingCardProjection`, `PendingCardPaintContext`, `transcriptAreaBeforePendingTail`, `noteCommittedFrame`: `src/core/app/app_render_runtime.zig:168-277`, `:2230-2238`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:289-307@481104fd`; `Shell::preview_room`, `preview_card`, `preview_landed`). A three-line prompt on a full 40-row screen previews its last line, five lines their last three, a 60-line pasted block its last 32 rows under the welcome (`p5-transcript-submit-multi`, `p5-submit-preview-*` wires; `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/submit-card-preview/`). The preview is the band's rows, not the document's, so none of it reaches scrollback, and every screen after it is the one the card was written onto before. The card's frame follows the preview's in the same tick, with nothing read and no resize taken between them, as fx writes the card in its next frame once its preview's has committed (`noteCommittedFrame`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:289-307@481104fd`; `Shell::take_preview_shown`). What stays open is a write that fails, as it does between fx's two writes: the card's frame refused or torn before its rows, or the preview's frame torn, and then a screen that shrinks before the retry -- the preview's rows the terminal moves into its history, or keeps above the band where they are the document's to xfx after a resize, stay there, and the card is still written once, whole and in order (`a_failed_write_and_a_shrink_before_the_retry_leave_only_what_the_shrink_took`). The room is measured against the last landed frame, as fx measures from its last committed one (`src/ui/transcript/runtime.zig:9929-9932@481104fd`; five lines and their Enter in one read preview what the one-row composer's frame left, `p5-submit-preview-oneread`). Checked against fx at 40x120, 30x100 and 24x80. Written whole at once, where fx would still preview (its pending path is shut only by an open stream, paced output, Ctrl-O or a turn start its worker will not hold, `installPendingSubmission`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:1123-1150@481104fd`), so xfx previews less than fx there, with no capture of fx's frame for them: a card under a line that does not end on a blank row (fx's mid-line endpoint, `pendingCardLeadingAdvanceRows`, `src/core/app/app_render_runtime.zig:209-218`); under a fork's `original session` line (`raw_gap`); under a tool group still open; one submitted while the document still owes rows, a reset's welcome among them; on a screen of 8 rows or fewer, whose running band leaves the preview no row (`room` 0, where fx may still paint one row at its band's bottom, `PendingCardPaintContext.init`, `:185-187`); a card whose preview the band loses the room for before the preview's frame (the slash menu opened by a key read with the Enter, a steer's banner): written whole that frame, the preview gone for good; a resize before the preview's frame (the preview is dropped); a card sent after a resize and before a band frame of the new size has landed, which leaves nothing to measure from (fx measures from its last committed frame, `runtime.zig:9929-9932`, then one of the old size); a question or Ctrl-O taking the other plane before the preview's frame: the card is paid whole before `1049h`, as the barrier pays every row the primary plane owes (fx paints no pending card while the alternate screen owns rendering, `:1264-1268`); and the exit. The activity row under it is xfx's own, as under every card (T7). A pasted prompt taller than the screen reaches scrollback whole, every line once and in order, as the session's first prompt too (433464dd, `p5-transcript-tallfirst`). A collapsed block is an **atomic entity**: it is a *span* of the draft rather than a name to be searched for, so exactly one run of the draft is the block and a second copy of its summary -- typed, or pasted back off the screen, before it or after it -- is only ever the words it looks like. Every motion steps over it whole (left, right, both word moves, and the two vertical moves, which land in front of it rather than inside it, as upstream's land on a unit's start), so the caret is never inside a summary; every delete that overlaps it at all takes the whole of it, so one backspace at its right edge, one forward delete at its left edge, a word delete or either kill removes the block rather than damaging its name. The span moves with the text around it as that text is edited. A number a block was given is **never** given again in one session -- a removed block's number is spent -- and a line recalled from history brings its blocks back under **fresh** numbers, allocated from the same checked counter a paste spends, which is what keeps two live blocks from answering to one name; at the end of that counter a paste is refused visibly and a recall hands the line back as words and says so, rather than wrapping or reusing a number. Removing a block gives its bytes back to the budget for good: what was pasted stops being sent, and typing the same words in again is typing rather than repairing. **A paste ends with its delivery, as fx v0.0.16's does.** A delivery is one continuously readable run of input: every read until a readiness check finds nothing more -- one read or many, since a read takes at most 128 bytes -- and across the 32-read cap of one turn, which carries it into the next turn unsettled (`event_loop.zig:94-119@481104fd`). Within one delivery, input that arrives behind the end marker is taken for the clipboard's rather than the user's, and it is **never read as keys**: it is counted and dropped, a second end marker or a new paste's start included. The paste it followed is **kept**, and upstream's `! input: Input that arrived right after the paste was ignored.` is said once it has been (`paste_framing.zig:58-68,102-107,159-165`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:168-198@481104fd`, 9e0eab41; c1db919 refused the whole paste instead). So clipboard text carrying an `ESC[201~` and a newline, delivered in one go, cannot submit the composer, answer an approval or take a question's choice. Every byte there is the clipboard's whatever its origin: a readiness check cannot tell a key a person pressed from a byte the terminal is still writing. **Past that, xfx diverges from fx, for security: the input stays quarantined until it has been quiet for 50 ms.** fx's delivery is the terminal's, not the clipboard's: a paste longer than the pty's input queue (1022 bytes on macOS) arrives as the terminal refills it, a check can find the queue empty in between, and fx settles the paste there and reads the rest of the clipboard as keys (`event_loop.zig:94-119@481104fd`). Measured against a real xfx at fx's boundary (the PASTE-GAP receipt, `measure.txt`): on a plain pty the paste's own newline submitted it in 2 of 3 runs at 64 KiB and at 1 MiB, and through tmux on a terminal that does not strip the marker in every run from 4 KiB up -- tmux ends a paste at its first embedded `ESC[201~` and forwards the rest as keys (`tty-keys.c:213-214,641-646@7f2a35ad`). And no terminal layer can be relied on to strip it: kitty, Alacritty, VTE, foot and Windows Terminal do; WezTerm strips once, so a nested marker survives; xterm, iTerm2 and tmux's `paste-buffer -S` let the user turn the filter off; Terminal.app cannot be checked (`terminals.md` there). So the paste is still settled where fx settles it -- the same `PasteEnd`, at the same check, so every capture is fx's -- and after it every byte is counted and dropped like the bytes behind the marker, until two checks 50 ms apart find nothing with nothing read between them: a fact about the queue, not the clock, so a loop that stalls between two reads of a paste still reads it as one. The warning is said at the first check after anything was dropped, and **while dropped input keeps arriving it is said again once a second**, in xfx's own words saying what holds the quarantine then (`Decoder::PASTE_REPEAT`, `Hold`): `! input: Keys ignored until input stops.` for the quiet, `! input: Paste still open; keys are ignored.` while a paste begun behind the end marker is open, and `! input: Key sequence open; keys are ignored.` while a marker is read past its first byte -- the two framing sentences name the cause and no release point, so they stay true however many keys continue the sequence and whatever ends the open paste; each of the three wraps into two rows of at most 18 columns, and fx's own warning into three, so each is whole on the review plane's three status rows down to the narrowest screen a session has, 20 columns, and a screen too short for all of its rows gives each of these four up whole rather than cut, because a cut of them says something else ("Input that arrived right after the", "Keys ignored until") -- every other status on the plane is given up from its last row, as before, so a refusal keeps the first clause that says which refusal it is for as long as there is a row for it; on that plane the sentence is the status row, and at the end of every turn's input -- after the burst, however it ended, and before the frame -- it is taken down once it no longer names what holds the quarantine: the hold has changed, the quarantine has ended, or a paste has replaced it with a new one in which nothing has been said; nothing new is said in its place, because the notices stay rate-limited and the plane's line is a view of now -- fx's own warning about input after a paste, and any other status, stay; and once a repeat has named a hold, a change in what holds the quarantine is said even with nothing dropped, so `ESC [` alone after the quiet's sentence is said in its own words -- at a check that finds nothing and at a turn's 32-read cap alike, so a stream that never pauses is said too (`Decoder::still`); on the plane the user can see, as the first warning is. fx has no such line because it has no quarantine.
