The panel **has the focus** while it is up: a `1` is an answer rather than a character in the composer, the caret sits on the choice Enter would take, and a key it does not bind reaches nothing -- including Ctrl-D, which does not end a session that is holding a turn open waiting to be told what to do. **Left and Alt-b mark the previous choice and Right and Alt-f the next**, while no amendment is open, as fx v0.0.16's do (its `.cursor_left`, `.word_left`, `.cursor_right`, `.word_right`; `routeApprovalEscapeAction`, `input_approval_runtime.zig:90-110@481104fd`), wrapping at both ends as Up and Down do (`moveChoice`, `approval_decision.zig:214-225`), and **every other spelling of the four leaves the choice where it is**, as fx's `else => {}` does (`:128`): Ctrl-, Alt-, Shift- and Super-Left and -Right, Ctrl-B and Ctrl-F, Shift-Alt-b and Shift-Alt-f -- to fx a composer move or a raw byte only an amendment reads (`modifiedArrowAction`, `escape_parser.zig:73-108`; `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:103-108`). Alt-f is `ESC f`, kitty's `CSI 102;3u` and xterm's `CSI 27;3;102~`. xfx's decoder keeps Alt-f apart from Ctrl-Right as `Action::AltF`, as `Action::AltB` keeps Alt-b, Shift-Left and Ctrl-B apart from Left as `Action::ComposerLeft` (and their mirrors as `ComposerRight`), and Super-Left as the line's start (`Action::LineStart`, COMPOSER-SUPER-ARROWS); the approval, the question and the full transcript read the event as it came, and the shell folds them back for the menus and the composer (`src/tui/shell.rs`'s `folded`). In an amendment every one of them is the caret's move, as fx's (`approvalDraftActionFromShortcut`, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:75-96`), Super-Left and -Right, whatever else is held, its start and end there (`line_start`/`line_end`, `:82-83`), which xfx's draft takes as Home and End (`p5-super-arrows-approval`, `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/composer-super-arrows/`; before COMPOSER-SUPER-ARROWS the step, and with Ctrl or Alt held too the word move), as are Ctrl-, Super- and Shift-Home and -End. `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/approval-horizontal-keys/` shows it (`p5-approval-horizontal-keys`, 29 snaps: every pair differs from fx only in the rows every edit's question differs in, the grant scope). **Up and Down move the choice, and every other spelling of them leaves it**, open amendment or not, as fx v0.0.16's do (`.cursor_up`/`.cursor_down`, `input_approval_runtime.zig:111-126@481104fd`): Shift-, Ctrl-, Alt- and Super-Up and -Down (kitty's `CSI 57352;<mask>u`/`57353` and the keypad's arrows included) are composer moves that reach `:128` (`modifiedArrowAction`, `escape_parser.zig:73-108`), `C-p` is the model-catalog action, which reaches the same `else` (`controlByteFeatureAction`, `escape_parser.zig:268-274`), and `C-n` a raw byte its question makes no action of (`approvalActionFromRawByte`, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:61-73`, `:103-108`). **In an amendment** the amendment stays open: Super-Up and -Down take the caret to the whole draft's start and end (`draft_start`/`draft_end` to `.cursor_home`/`.cursor_end`, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:82-83`; `cursorHome`/`cursorEnd`, `approval_amendment.zig:84-92`), and the other ten do nothing (`approvalDraftActionFromShortcut` names no `visual_up`/`paragraph_up`, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:86`). xfx's decoder keeps each apart from the arrow, in fx's order but for Shift beside Ctrl -- Super as `Action::DraftStart`/`DraftEnd` (Shift remembered), then Alt as `ParagraphUp`/`ParagraphDown` (Shift or Ctrl or not), then Shift as `SelectUp`/`SelectDown` (Ctrl or not), then Ctrl as `ComposerUp`/`ComposerDown`; fx's table reads Ctrl before Shift and gives both `visual_up`, Shift's selection apart, which only a question's choice reads (`src/ui/input/runtime.zig:184-189@481104fd`), and since the approval tells only Super from the rest, any order with Super first gives fx's result there -- and the approval, the question and the full transcript read the event as it came, as do the menus and the composer, each as fx's move (the held Up and Down above; `src/tui/shell.rs`'s `arrow_to_pickers` and `Shell::act`). A key that does nothing at the question is swallowed before the panel is asked, so the choice, the draft and the readiness receipt are as they were, and an Enter read with it, in the same buffer or with no frame between, answers the choice the last frame marked (before, Shift-Down then Enter with no frame between answered `Always` under a frame that marked `Apply once`). On the review plane `C-p` and `C-n` still walk the change (below). `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/approval-vertical-spellings/` shows it (`p5-approval-vertical-keys`, 27 snaps: every pair differs from fx only in the rows every edit's question differs in, the grant scope). **Not changed, and shown by APPROVAL-HORIZONTAL-KEYS' `probe/`**: `ESC ESC [ D` at a choice cancels the turn, where fx's is a composer move that leaves the choice -- xfx's decoder has no meta prefix and reads Escape, then Left -- and `ESC ESC [ A`/`[ B` the same (owner **META-PREFIX-DECODE**, queued: the decoder has no meta prefix, and the gap reaches the question, the approval and the composer alike; the question's own fold spellings, which QUESTION-LEFT-SPELLINGS was proposed for, MODIFIED-ARROWS-SCREENS closed: the question reads the event as the decoder spelled it). **Also not changed, found by this unit**: a bracketed paste into a draft keeps its line breaks and tabs, where fx's turns each into a space (`normalizeApprovalAmendmentPasteInPlace`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:19-25@481104fd`), so an xfx draft can hold more than one line, and on it Home and End -- and Super-Left and -Right and Shift-Home and -End, which are Home and End to fx -- are a line's edges where Super-Up and -Down and Ctrl- and Super-Home and -End are the whole draft's (on fx, whose draft is one line, they are the same edges; `super_left_right_and_the_draft_edges_in_an_amendment_are_fxs`); owner unit proposed, APPROVAL-PASTE-SPACES. **A capture artifact, not a divergence, found by COMPOSER-SUPER-ARROWS: one amendment pair differs in a caret cell neither side omits.** In `p5-super-arrows-approval`'s `amend-super-right` -- a letter appended at the end of a ten-character draft -- fx and xfx each write exactly one reverse-video blank after the `y` (`ESC[7m` `ESC[0m`, read off both binaries' wire, `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/composer-super-arrows/round2/caret-wire.txt`), but fx's `capture-pane -e` row ends in the `ESC[7m` with its blank trimmed and the reset carried to the next row, where xfx's keeps the blank, and the M1 comparison (`scripts/fxcap/cells.py`'s `parse`) adds a cell only for a character. The comparison is left as it is: over this unit's 47-scenario sweeps the class touches that one pair (`round2/trim-scan.txt`); a comparator that gave a row-final reverse, background or underline SGR its one styled blank is proposed as its own unit. **When an affirmative may be taken: fx v0.0.16's one condition, and stricter.** At a file approval fx takes none while a modal frame is pending: its check opens `if (decision != .deny and app.approval_prompt.request.?.file != null)` (`input_approval_runtime.zig:191-192`), then `settledForApproval` (`render_request.zig:172-177`; `reasonsAffectApproval`, `:367-370`), and a refused key is spent, a frame asked for and nothing said but a trace line (`input_approval_runtime.zig:207-217`); every key that moves the choice or a draft's caret, types into the draft or edits it asks for that frame (`input_approval_runtime.zig:86`, `:98`, `:108`, `:117`, `:125`; `app_input_runtime.zig:1251-1255`), and the request clears only when a frame commits whole (`beginAttempt`, `render_request.zig:272-296`; `:298-324`; `app_render_runtime.zig:1002-1032`). xfx copies that condition at its readiness layer (`src/tui/approval_readiness.rs`'s `Readiness::changed`): a key the question answers by moving the marker, editing a draft (a letter, C-u, C-w, a paste's end) or walking the review plane further is a change, and no `1`, `2` or Enter on either plane is taken until a frame composed after it has landed -- reconciled on the next tick, or at once when the band wrote nothing because the screen already held it (a move undone in the same read); the refused key is spent, the question stays up with the change drawn, and nothing is said, as fx says nothing -- the next Enter answers what that frame shows. Keys the question does not bind hold nothing back; a draft edit that changes nothing -- Backspace or C-u in an empty draft, Left at the draft's start -- still holds the Enter until the next frame, as fx's does (Backspace and C-u by the raw-byte route, `input_approval_runtime.zig:59-61`, `approval_decision.zig:152-155`, `:170-174`; Left by `input_approval_runtime.zig:86`), and so does Tab in an open draft, which changes nothing and asks for the frame, as fx's `.redraw` does (`approval_decision.zig:142-143`), where xfx took the Enter at once. Ctrl-L at a permission question does nothing, as fx's: no approval action is made of the byte (`approvalActionFromRawByte`, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:98-108`; `shortcuts.zig:24`, `:31-41`), so the question takes it and asks for nothing (`app_input_runtime.zig:1173-1184`), where xfx repainted the screen whole as damaged and kept the receipt until that frame. A refusal -- `3`, Esc, Ctrl-C, Enter on choice 3 -- is answered at once, as fx's `decision != .deny`; so the feedback is held back for the affirmatives only, and Enter on choice 3 read with a draft edit sends that draft with the refusal unseen, as fx's does. **Stricter than fx: every approval, not only file approvals** -- the dispatcher's decision (2026-11-10, reversible, kept for the user's veto). A run-command request carries `command` and no `file` (`permission_request.zig:39-40`), so at a command fx's Down and Enter in one read grant `Always` under a frame that marked `Apply once` (`p5-approval-readiness-settled`'s `cmd2-down-enter`), and a grant widened past what the frame showed is a safety matter on any approval. To restrict the condition to file approvals as fx does, `Shell::approval_changed` would call `Readiness::changed` only while the question's subject is a path. `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/approval-readiness-settled/` shows it (`p5-approval-readiness-settled`: the edit's and the write's pairs identical to fx's up to the grant scope's rows). **The draft's keys are fx v0.0.16's** (APPROVAL-DRAFT-KEYS, `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/approval-draft-keys/`), but for four differences each owned by a unit: Super-Left and -Right as a step (COMPOSER-SUPER-ARROWS, above), a paste's line breaks and tabs kept (APPROVAL-PASTE-SPACES, above), and Ctrl+Shift+A/B/E/F (COMPOSER-SELECTION-EXTEND) and the tilde Home and End (DECODER-TILDE-HOME-END), below. fx's set: by the raw-byte route C-a, C-e, C-b, C-f, C-k, C-u and C-w (`fromFocusedEditorControlByte`, `src/ui/input/shortcuts.zig:31-41@481104fd`) and Backspace (`src/ui/input/runtime.zig:66`); by the decoded route Left and Right in each spelling, Home and End, Super-Up and -Down, the word moves (Alt-b, Alt-f, Ctrl- and Alt-Left and -Right), Delete, Alt-Backspace, Alt-d, Ctrl- and Alt-Delete, Super-Delete and Super-Backspace (`approvalDraftActionFromShortcut`, `:75-96`; `shortcuts.zig:43-65`); a typed character and a paste. **xfx's draft no longer undoes, redoes or yanks**, because fx's has none of the three: C-y and C-_ are no focused-editor key and no approval byte (`shortcuts.zig:22-23`, `:31-41`; `runtime.zig:61-73`, `:103-108`), a kitty or xterm Ctrl+Y or Ctrl+_ is that byte replayed (`escape_parser.zig:255-259`; `app_input_runtime.zig:746-756`), and Super+Z and Super+Shift+Z are a composer undo and redo no draft action is made of (`escape_parser.zig:232-234`; `input_approval_runtime.zig:128`). So in a draft each does nothing and holds no Enter back, where xfx's undid, redid and yanked, and C-w then C-y sent `ab cd` where fx sends `ab `. **Still apart, found by this unit**: Ctrl+Shift+A, B, E and F, a selection-extending move to fx, move fx's draft caret (`escape_parser.zig:235-242`; `runtime.zig:79-83`) and bind nothing in xfx's, as in its composer (COMPOSER-SELECTION-EXTEND's); fx reads `CSI 7 ~` and `CSI 8 ~` as Home and End and `CSI 1 ; <mod> ~` and `CSI 4 ; <mod> ~` as the line's or the draft's edge (`escape_parser.zig:628-635`, `:699-707`), each a draft edge there, and xfx's decoder reads them as nothing, in the draft as in the composer (owner unit proposed, DECODER-TILDE-HOME-END). **C-j answers a permission question as Enter does** (APPROVAL-CTRL-J, `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/approval-ctrl-j/`), where xfx's did nothing: fx makes `'\r', '\n'` an approval's `.submit` (`approvalActionFromByte`, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:64@481104fd`), its focused-editor route keeping no newline (`shortcuts.zig:26`, `:31-41`), and a kitty or xterm report of Ctrl+J, whatever else is held, is that byte replayed (`escape_parser.zig:255-258`, `:740-752`; a press or a repeat, `:774-777`). So at a choice and in a draft, on the band and on the review plane, C-j answers the marked choice and sends its draft, through the same readiness check as Enter -- a move or a draft edit read with it waits for the frame -- and on choice 3 it is a refusal, answered at once. A menu open under the question does not see it: the question has the keys, as fx's approval takes the byte before any menu does (`app_input_runtime.zig:1161-1216`; the picker's C-j yields to it, `:1110-1116`). **The newline habit, as in fx**: C-j is the composer's newline, and once a question's frame is up the question takes every key, so a user writing a multi-line prompt while a turn runs who presses C-j for a new line after a question has appeared answers it -- `Apply once` at choice 1 -- where xfx swallowed that C-j; so does a C-j typed for a new line in a draft, which sends the draft as it stands, and an LF a terminal sends for a modified Enter. The readiness check holds such a C-j back only while the question's frame has not landed, or within the read of a change it has not shown. fx does the same: the raw byte reaches `approvalActionFromByte` whatever the user meant by it. To keep a newline's C-j from answering, a departure from fx and the user's to make, `Shell::decide` would drop `Action::ControlJ` from its one arm (`Input::Action(Action::Submit | Action::ControlJ)`). Not copied here, left to a unit of its own: fx's model question takes C-j as Enter too (`questionActionFromByte`, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:141`), where xfx's does nothing (QUESTION-CTRL-J, proposed). fx's `/permissions` rule confirmation is an approval prompt (`submitPermissionChoice`, `input_approval_runtime.zig:185-188`), and xfx's reads the approval's keys too, C-j among them (RULE-CONFIRM-KEYS; the `/permissions` row). It takes the composer's rows and the document's above them, as upstream's question replaces its footer: the bottom rule and the hint row stay where they were, and the draft the composer holds is kept, unpainted, until the answer gives the rows back. The question travels as an event and the answer on the **control** channel -- unbounded, and drained inside the turn -- so an answer cannot queue behind a prompt the turn will not dequeue until it ends, and an Esc typed at a panel both refuses the question and stops the turn behind it. **Esc and Ctrl-C are not the same refusal**, and the panel is where the difference is easiest to miss: Ctrl-C answers *this call* -- no, and the turn goes on with a prompt still queued behind it -- while Esc cancels the turn, so it travels as the interrupt's cancellation, carries the same watermark, prints the same two sentences, stops the turn and drops what was waiting. The refusal comes back *with* the cancellation rather than instead of it: the prompter is the thing parked on that channel, and it answers the question `no` and hands the cancellation on to the loop that can act on it. Nothing on the question is cut: the path, the command and the scope wrap onto the rows they need, and a choice a narrow screen clips is a choice the readiness gate refuses. **The model can also ask a question of its own, and it is not an approval.** `ask_user_question` takes the same rows of the band and the same focus and shares neither vocabulary nor authority with the permission panel: one question of the batch at a time, the model's options by **absolute** ordinal so the number on a row is the number to type whatever the window is showing, and a synthetic `Other` slot appended last -- identified by index rather than by its label -- which only opens a draft when it is taken, so a digit is an ordinal at a choice and a character inside the draft. Tab walks the batch and keeps each draft, the answers come back in question order however the batch was walked, and none of the three ways out mints an authority. Escape and Ctrl-C both stop the turn the batch belongs to, as fx's `cancelQuestionPrompt` does (`input_question_runtime.zig:59-81@481104fd`; `tui/shell.rs:9718-9727`) -- except that Escape at a freeform draft with text in it first arms a clear, on both sides (`app_input_runtime.zig:2690-2710@481104fd`; `tui/shell.rs:9786-9797`): Escape stops the turn only at an empty field. When a key stops it, the document keeps `▪ Cancelled`, the call answers `aborted by user`, which the model reads in the next prompt's request, the sentinel never reaches the wire (`scripts/smoke-tui.sh:10483-10498`, `:10535-10545`), and the calls queued behind the question in that step never run. Nothing shown is ever the answer: the row is clipped for the screen and it is the canonical label, encoded and never truncated, that goes back to the model. **A change whose review outgrows the band is reviewed on a screen of its own**, the surface upstream reviews every file on. Which surface a question takes is a property of the change rather than of the terminal: a **content** mutation whose review is more than twelve rows takes the alternate buffer -- an `edit_file` and a `write_file` both carry the part of the file the change touches, the changed lines and five unchanged ones either side with where they sit in the file, so the review numbers its lines, shows the ones around the change and counts the rest, however far past the 64 KiB bound of a side the change is -- and every other question -- a command, a directory, a change the band shows whole -- is answered in the band with the document still above it; on a screen too short for the band's question a change goes to the plane rather than being refused. A rule keyed on the screen's height alone would review a one-word edit on its own screen the moment somebody made their window short. What that screen renders is bounded and made inert at the permission boundary rather than at the surface: each side is escaped and then truncated at a character boundary to at most 64 KiB, with one ellipsis inside the bound, so the review carries at most 128 KiB and nothing in it can command or reorder the terminal. **One character is kept rather than named, and it is the change's own structure**: a real line break survives the boundary and the channel, and the review screen turns each one into a row, so a twenty-line change is twenty rows rather than one wrapped run and the second half is reached by walking. Everything else a terminal would act on is **named**, and named *individually*: the three whitespace controls by their own spellings (`\\t`, `\\r`, and `\\n` in the line shell's one-line quotation) and every other control by its code point (`\\u{001B}` for an `ESC`), so a payload that swapped one control for another is two screens rather than one. The review screen names the bidirectional overrides the same way, since those are neither controls nor text. fx v0.0.16's review writes fx's lossier forms instead (`\\x1b`, a tab and a single backslash kept as themselves, a trailing carriage return dropped); xfx's stays, since a change of a carriage return alone would read there as no change (`review-diff-spelling`, `scripts/fxcap/parity-ledger.toml`), while its Ctrl-O screen, which decides nothing, writes fx's (the full transcript row). The split happens before anything is painted, so a break never reaches a row it did not end. The rendering is also **injective**, in two ways that were each a collision first: the backslash an escape spends is itself escaped, so a file whose lines really end and a file that merely contains the two characters `\\` and `n` are two different screens; and a control is named by its own code point rather than blanked to one symbol, so a file of `ESC` and a file of `BEL` are two different screens as well. Without the first, replacing a hundred-line file with one line of literal escapes was shown as a change of nothing; without the second, so was replacing every control byte in a file with a different one. The review opens at its tail, against the controls, as upstream's does, and `C-p`/`C-n` walk it back toward its head and forward again -- upstream walks it with the wheel, and xfx asks for no mouse reports, so the hint row says `ctrl+p/n scroll` where upstream's says `wheel scroll` -- because a screen with no way past its first screenful would be showing the head of a change and calling it the change; the arrows still walk the three choices, since a key that scrolled *and* chose would answer by accident, and both surfaces decide which key means which answer with the same function. **The plane is taken in one frame and given back in one write**: entering carries `1049h`, an erase and the whole surface, and leaving carries `1049l`, the hidden cursor and the complete band repaint in a single `write_all`, so no terminal has an intermediate blank screen to present; ownership moves only after those bytes are delivered, and **the exit that can ask, asks the band rather than the session**: the normal shutdown reads which buffer the terminal was really left on -- a record of delivered bytes, not of intent -- and writes `1049l` only if it was the alternate one, because a reset written by a session that never took that buffer would swap in, on a terminal that models one, a screen its user was not looking at. The panic hook and the `SIGTERM`/`SIGHUP` handlers **cannot** ask: they run where a lock may not be taken and a state may not be consulted, so they write one compile-time-constant restore that leads with `?2026l` and then `1049l`, both **unconditionally**. Every restore, normal and abnormal, closes a synchronized update before it does anything else -- a torn write whose cleanup the terminal refused leaves one open, and a terminal handed back inside it would show nothing new until its own timeout -- as upstream's restores both do (`src/core/app/app_lifecycle.zig:41`, `:44` at `c1db919`); the normal exit's own check refuses a restore that does not. That is the trade the two paths make in opposite directions, and it is deliberate: an exit that knows what is on the screen is precise, and an exit that cannot know is defensive -- a stray `1049l` on a normal exit would be a bug, while a missing one on a crash would leave the user on a review screen belonging to a process that no longer exists. An alternate frame updates nothing the band knows about the *normal* buffer, so the row the exit erases from is still the row the band painted there, and the shell's own output above it survives the excursion. Upstream's **amendment draft** is here too, on both surfaces and with the same keys: Tab on the first or third choice opens that answer's own draft, on the choice's own row after its words as upstream's is (`❯ 1  Apply once, use capitals`) (`2` has none -- what it buys is keyed by tool and target and cannot carry a condition), the two drafts are independent, the arrows end editing and move the choice while keeping both buffers (`C-p`, `C-n` and the modified Up and Down leave both, Super-Up and -Down taking the caret to the draft's edges, above) -- and only the draft being typed is painted, as upstream's is (`approval_ui.zig:1181-1193@481104fd`): the one left behind is kept unpainted, and Tab on its choice paints it again. **So a choice can carry a draft that no frame shows, and Enter or that choice's digit sends it** with the answer, painted or not, as upstream's `materializeResponse` does (`approval_decision.zig:112-127`): type a reason on `1`, step to `2` and back, and `1` or Enter answers yes-once with that reason though the row reads `Apply once`. xfx painted a draft left behind until APPROVAL-HORIZONTAL-KEYS, a difference this row never recorded; copying upstream here is a reversible decision kept for the user's veto (one condition in `Drafts::blocks`, `src/tui/approval_amendment.rs`). As upstream's too, a digit is a character inside a draft and an answer outside one, and a bracketed paste is assembled by the draft itself -- bounded at 4 KiB on the raw **and** the decoded size, refused whole rather than truncated, kept with what followed its end marker in the same delivery ignored (the composer's rule, above), and never collapsed into the entity summary the composer would mint, because a sentence the model read as `[Pasted text #1, 40 lines]` would be a summary standing in for text the user believes they sent. **The sentence is context and never authority**: an allow with an amendment runs the model's original arguments, a denial with one still writes nothing, and nothing reads it to widen, narrow or re-key a grant. Enter sends **only** the draft belonging to the answer it takes and discards the other; Escape and Ctrl-C are the fail-safe exits and send none at all, which is the one place they differ from `3`. It reaches the model as a user message **after** every tool result of its step, and it is recorded as a `tool_feedback` frame keyed by call id -- not as a `user_message`, which would open a turn in the middle of a tool batch. Opening, closing or growing a draft revokes the readiness receipt and the next disclosed frame re-earns it; the draft is paid for out of what is left after the target, the controls and the scope, so it can shorten only itself. **The sentence is read back in the transcript** as upstream reads it back, the user's own prompt card -- `┃ ` and the sentence in bold, a row per line of it, and a blank row under it (`write_user_prompt`, `app_worker_runtime.zig:1020-1026`) -- under the tool group's rows, live and on a resumed session alike, and a prompt in the full transcript (C-o). It is written when the runtime reports the delivery rather than when the key was pressed, so the document can never claim something was said that was not: an interrupted draft is on the screen while it is being typed and reaches neither the transcript, the journal nor the wire. That is the one place it can differ from upstream's: upstream writes the card as soon as that call's own result is in (`appendPermissionFeedbackAfterToolResult`, `orchestrator.zig:5360-5380`, from `:11379-11387`), before the step's later calls have run, and xfx delivers every sentence of a step after its last result, so in a step of several calls the card comes after the step's last result rather than its own call's; in a step of one call the two are the same place. A `write_file` carries the same bounded before/after an edit does, built in the executor from the preimage it has already read a complete-read proof for and the exact text that would replace it, so the largest change this product makes is reviewed as a change rather than as a digest; a target with nothing at it is reviewed as all additions, `-0` in the header, and one holding bytes that are not UTF-8 says what they are and how many rather than being rendered lossily as replacement characters. `create_folder` changes no content and still has no pair to show. Off this surface nothing changes: a run with no terminal on both ends still has no approval channel and still denies every `ask`-mode mutation with the refusal a pipe gets. A panic inside a turn never touches the terminal: the runtime thread neither restores it nor reports into it -- the panic arrives as data, and the UI restores, prints it **once**, and exits nonzero. **The TUI answers the same slash commands the line-oriented shell does, and no others**, from that shell's own `classify` and its `SLASH_REGISTRY`, so a leading `/` cannot mean two different things depending on which front end it was typed into: `/help`, `/version`, `/quit` and `/stats` are answered by the UI thread, and `/status` and `/copy` from what the runtime publishes; `/model` and `/setup` are handed to the runtime thread as work, because the model, the conversation and the provider they change live there -- and each of them has to change *between* turns rather than under one; `/clear`, `/new` and `/reset` are one reset, sent as a control message that cancels the running turn and drops the conversation once it has unwound, so a reset never waits behind a turn or takes a queue place from the prompt typed after it; `/permissions` and Shift-Tab are answered by the UI thread and hand their change to the runtime on a channel of their own, which it applies between pieces of work. A name that is not one of the seven is refused with the same sentence that shell refuses it with and is **not** sent to the model, so a mistyped command does not quietly become a prompt. A reset's screen half is upstream's scrollback handoff (`commitVisibleTranscriptBeforeFreshSession`): the band's rows are erased, the document's rows still on the screen are scrolled off the top into the terminal's own scrollback -- `CUP` to the bottom row and one linefeed per row, the same instrument every append uses, and not the blank row the document ends on, which upstream does not carry either -- and the welcome line is written on row 1 of the blank screen with the band floated under it; a question that owns the other buffer defers it until the plane is given back. Answer rows that had already landed when a running turn was reset go to scrollback with the rest of the document, where upstream, which holds an answer in its live region until it is committed, carries only the prompt card. A draft that is one word beginning with `/` also opens an **inline completion menu** in the same rows of the band a question would take -- never both, because a question closes a menu before it appears. The menu is a view of the composer rather than a second focus: the caret stays in the text being typed, only five keys are taken out of the stream (Up, Down, Tab, Enter, Esc; and `C-k` and `C-j` as Up and Down, except under the full transcript, which holds over the menu and where they are the composer's -- `routePickerControlByte`, `app_input_runtime.zig:1093-1116@481104fd`; `p5-ctrlo-slash`, CTRLO-OTHER-PICKERS), and Enter runs the line rather than taking the marked row, so no command costs two Returns. Matches are ranked name-prefix, then alias-prefix, then substring, ties broken by the order `/help` lists them, so the rows are a pure function of what was typed; Tab completes the marked command, with a trailing space for the one that takes an argument; Esc closes the menu without arming the composer's own double-Escape clear, and the closing survives further letters of the same word. The skills the query finds follow its commands, and a slash word that ends a longer draft has the rest of a command's name painted after the caret instead, for Right or Tab to take (the `/skills` row). Upstream's fuzzy matching, its categories and its argument completion are absent. The word has to start the draft: fx opens the menu for a draft whose first character past any leading spaces, tabs or line breaks is `/` (`inlinePickerTriggerKind`, `src/core/input/picker_state.zig:293-294@481104fd`; `slashCompletionPrefix`, `command_specs.zig:568-576`), where xfx's `Trigger::of` asks for the `/` as the first character (`src/tui/picker.rs:165-167`), so `  /he` or a slash word after a blank line opens fx's menu and not xfx's. Uncaptured; owner SLASH-LEADING-WS (found by PAGE-KEYS-SETTINGS-COLUMN's review, not fixed there). **Every line the composer submits is remembered, and walking back through them never costs the line being typed.** A hundred entries, newest first, with adjacent repeats folded into one and commands kept beside prompts -- a command is a line the user typed, and running one again is the commonest reason to reach for a recall; a blank line is the one submission that is not recorded, because there is nothing to come back to. Up and Down are the composer's own movement first, take the caret to the draft's start or end at its first or last visual row, and become the walk only from there (`input_completion_runtime.zig:265-284`), so a multi-row draft is still navigable by arrow, and `C-n` steps forward from wherever the caret is (upstream's `history_next`, `shortcuts.zig:17`). `C-p` is not the backward walk: it is upstream's model catalog (`open_model_catalog`, `escape_parser.zig:271`), the menu `/model` opens, with the draft lent out of the composer -- through the effort column a choice in it goes on into -- and given back as it was, text, blocks and caret, when the flow ends: a model applied, Escape, a second `C-p`, `C-c` or `C-d` (`app_input_runtime.zig:2356-2404`, `:1666-1676`, `:2775-2779`); over the `/help` catalog or the `/statusline` picker it does nothing (`:2406-2418`). A recalled line that begins with `/` opens no completion menu until it is edited, so a further Up keeps walking (`picker_state.zig:451-466`). Entering the walk **captures the half-typed line**, and stepping forward past the newest entry hands it back, so a recall is not a gesture that destroys what was being written. Neither end wraps: past the oldest line is nothing, and the near end is the captured draft. An edit ends the walk and the edited text is what the next one comes back to; a caret move does not, because reading a recalled prompt before stepping further back is exactly what an arrow key is for there. Nothing is written to disk and nothing survives the process: a session recall rather than a history file, which is the boundary the `prompt history` row records. An entry carries the collapsed pastes its summaries named, so a recalled line comes back as the **blocks** rather than as words -- under fresh paste numbers, which is upstream's renumbering and is what keeps the recalled line and the draft it replaced from both answering to `#1`. The draft a walk stands aside carries its blocks the same way, so the half-typed line the walk comes back to is the whole of it. This phase adds no command, no tool and no canonical slash name -- it adds one alias, `/exit`, on the `/quit` row. **One thing is named here because it is absent from every stable release.** A stateless per-vector output check -- every vector the TUI is about to write decoded into a model of the terminal and compared, **before** `write_all` rather than after it, against an intent its emitter declares separately from the bytes: the whole model as a value (plane, cursor visibility, the shown title and the title stack, each mode as a tri-state that distinguishes *nothing has said* from a guessed default, whether scrollback was erased, the queries asked, the saved cursor), the cells against a grid built from the emitter's own inputs with colour compared through a parser of its own on each side, and a document append replayed in emission order so a row that scrolls off the top is compared **before** the scroll evicts it -- is **absent from stable releases**; preview-channel builds run it unconditionally, with no opt-in flag (`.prd/03-tui-port.md` item 21). **The transport those vectors go out on is absent from stable releases in the same way**: preview-channel builds write every byte of a session through one unbuffered counted sink -- no buffer and no flush, so there is no vector a session believes it wrote that is still held in one -- which adds up what the kernel accepted and tells a refusal and a write that took *nothing* (the screen is as it was; the vector is still owed, and the half-second budget above is what ends a run of them) apart from a write the screen took **part** of (it is not, so the session ends on it at once, on a terminal the exit still puts back, rather than offering the same vector again and writing that prefix twice). That is **containment**: an accepted count is a kernel receipt rather than a terminal's acknowledgement, and nothing here claims a parser recovered. Partial-write recovery covers the two repaints whose whole intended content is in hand -- the primary band's frame, and a repaint of the alternate plane a question already owns -- each with one fixed cleanup and one whole rebuild of the same repaint in the same call, never a replay of its bytes, and no approval readiness until a later write the session verifies on its own; every other `Partial` -- a plane transition, a document append or carry, a reset's scrollback handoff, the theme query, the retint -- is contained and fatal -- the session ends and leaves its give-up diagnostic -- and its bytes are never replayed (the table under this one gives each path and its reason), though a `Rejected`/`ZeroProgress` write -- an append or a refused handoff alike -- is retained and reoffered under the existing budget rather than dropped. A released binary has none of it (`.prd/03-tui-port.md` item 21; [`.prd/06-qa-harness.md`](../.prd/06-qa-harness.md) rows 3c, 3d and 3e drive the containment and both recoveries on a real pseudoterminal with the fault build). Without it -- and for any other value of it, for every subcommand, and whenever either end is not a terminal -- xfx is the line-oriented shell of the `interactive` row and changes no terminal state. **Widths a terminal disputes, in an answer and in the composer.** The painter counts a scalar a terminal was measured drawing wider than the width table counted it, where fx counts it wider too, as fx counts it (3,838 of them, `src/tui/wrap.rs`'s `FX_COUNTED`; the same cells as fx's in `p5-ctrlo-wider-inline`). Regional indicators are counted as fx counts them, which is not as the terminals draw them: a pair that is no flag four cells (drawn two), a lone one two (one on tmux), and a run a grapheme cluster at a time where fx pairs it along the line (Z U S six, fx four). A document row ends early and loses nothing; where the grid rewrites only the cells that changed, a cell the count put elsewhere can stay on the screen (`ab` of an erased `🇦🇦abcd` in the composer, fx's and xfx's alike) -- the ledger's cause `ri-run-pairing`, RI-COUNT's. The 43 fx and xfx count alike and a terminal draws wider (`src/tui/drawn_wider.rs`) are **named in an answer's rows** -- prose, a code panel and its label, a table's cells, operational text; a base with a skin-tone modifier is named past the base and its modifier stays (`\u{261d}🏻`, whole on both terminals); display only, the journal and `/copy`'s reply keep the model's bytes -- where fx writes them as themselves and its rows lose glyphs on tmux 3.7b and libghostty-vt 1.3.2 (`answer-drawn-wider-named`, unsafe-to-copy; `p5-ctrlo-wider-inline-fam`, `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/ctrlo-wider/`). **The draft is written as fx writes it** and loses glyphs as fx's does (`y a` of `fam ☝ ⸺ 🖐 x` `U+E0002` `y a` `U+FE0F` `b` on tmux, four `U+261D` in front costing its tail): the user's own text, a decision the ledger's cause `composer-drawn-wider` records, owned by COMPOSER-WIDER (display-only naming with a caret map; a file name an `@` completion puts in the draft is the route a file's text could take, PLAUSIBLE). The card of a sent prompt is the user's text too, written as it stands as a document row, where every glyph stays (fx's card loses `y a` to the anchor after `a` `U+FE0F`: `inline-band-row-anchor`). |

 | `@` file picker | ui | partial | Upstream's `@` picker (`src/core/workspace/file_index.zig`, `src/core/input/file_picker_path.zig`, `src/core/app/input_completion_runtime.zig:779-850`) in the band under the composer, its rows starting under the `@`, the marked row on a grey bar with the matched letters bold, and the hint row left as it is (upstream's hint switch has no file-picker arm, `paint_plan.zig:946-978`). A query is fx v0.0.16's case-blind subsequence ranked by its placement score (unit AT-RANK): fzf's v2 path score of the best placement -- 16 a letter, the first letter's bonus doubled, 9 after a `/`, 8 at another word start and on punctuation, 7 at a camel or digit start, a run keeping its first letter's bonus and at least 4, a gap 3 and 1 more for each position past its first -- the earliest of the best, and past 4096 cells the leftmost (`scoreAlignment`, `alignBest`, `file_index.zig:1410-1423`, `:1498-1521`, `:1527-1720@481104fd`); placed inside the name with 4 more, or from the name's own folder into it, and failing both anywhere, below every name match (`choosePlacement`, `:1776-1819`); ordered an exact match first -- the path, the name, or the name before a dot -- then a placement that ends in the name, its score, its earlier start, the shorter path and bytes, files and folders alike, an added directory's entries by their absolute paths (`exactFit`, `scoreBetter`, `rankedCandidateBetter`, `:1753-1758`, `:1884-1899`); the bold letters are that placement (`reconstructMatchSpans`, `:1921-1983`), and a browsed folder's names are ranked by the same score (`NameQuery`, `path_completion.zig:56`, `:93`, `:234`); at most 32 rows (`file_completion_state.zig:7`), kept as the index is searched, a path placed only as far as it could still enter them, on its lower case, name and letters kept from the walk (unit AT-RANK-PERF; `rankTopN`, `fillProgressive`, `file_index.zig:1330-1403`, `:209-222@481104fd`). Case is folded by Unicode lower-casing where fx's uses simple case folding, so `ς` and `σ` match each other only in fx (`file_index.zig:2909-2944@481104fd`): unit CASE-FOLD-SIMPLE, queued. A path-shaped one (`@src/`, `@~/`, `@../`) lists that directory on a worker instead, the folder itself as its first row so it can be chosen from inside (7c8ef4a3; `listedFolder`, `path_completion.zig:133-143@481104fd`; none for a partial name, the root, or a folder that cannot be painted), and the frame of the keystroke that starts the listing waits up to 8 ms for its rows, so `indexing files...` does not flash first; a listing that outlasts the wait lets that frame go and the next listing's frame goes at once, until one lands inside 8 ms, the loop checking back every millisecond meanwhile (09d2e934, 6aa678d9; `FileListingWait`, `picker_state.zig:99-139`; `main.zig:191-207`, `:3007`). What waits is what fx's held `flushRequestedFrame` holds -- the band's or the full transcript's update, the document rows it carries, and `/sound`'s bells, which fx flushes inside it after the frame (`app_render_runtime.zig:1059-1063`) and xfx rings after the frame that releases the hold; the program status report and the window title (then written on its own) go out on their tick, as fx writes them outside its frame (`app_worker_runtime.zig:762-791`, `app_session_runtime.zig:2958-2987`). **No lookup is asked for the list while it is not eligible** -- a question, an approval or a rule change's confirmation up, the full transcript up, or a catalog menu owning the surface (the catalog, the settings menu, the skills menu while it is shown, `/model`'s menu, the session picker), fx's `filePickerEligible` term for term where xfx has the surface (`input_completion_runtime.zig:754-786`; fx's auth picker has no counterpart, `/login` being deferred): the list asks for no listing and no index refresh, Tab does not retry, and no frame waits (`prepareFilePicker`, `:848-853`; `:949`) -- the index's own work goes on: a refresh queued before then starts when the running walk ends, and a `/workspace` change walks its new roots, as fx's join and install do; a listing in flight is told to stop -- its thread asks where fx's does, before each advance, after it and before an entry's `stat`, and cannot interrupt a filesystem call already under way (`path_completion.zig:43-146`); an advance that fails fails the listing, as fx's does -- and its answer is never taken; once the list is eligible again its lookup, with the index refresh a list asked for meanwhile, runs once (`directory_completion_job.zig:140-151`). Up/Down and `C-k`/`C-j` move the bar, PgUp/PgDn page it by the composer's row cap (fx's `inputRowLimit`: 19 rows on a 41-row screen, `fx-p5-input-row-limit-41x120-at-pgdn`), both wrapping, and the window scrolls only past the rows the last frame that landed painted the list in (99e62b8e; `navigate`, `file_completion_state.zig:239-249`; `paintedFilePickerReceipt`, `app_render_runtime.zig:2355-2360`): six until a frame paints one; a frame paints the rows that keep the bar in view from where the last move left the window and leaves it there, a refresh that finds the marked path or empties the list included (`paint_plan.zig:936-937`, `file_completion_state.zig:258-275`, `:293`), so on a screen made shorter the next move scrolls by the rows it shows now; Tab and Enter put the path in, `@path ` for a file either way; on a folder Enter mentions it -- `@dir/ `, any quote closed (`@"./space dir/" `), and the list closes -- and Tab opens it, `@dir/` and the listing goes on (7c8ef4a3; `applyFilePickerSelection`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:957`, `:993-1012`, `:1042`); Enter never submits the same keystroke; Escape closes it for that word. **The keys are held behind a query-and-visibility gate** (fx's `trusted`, `file_completion_state.zig:104`, `:218-240`): a new or changed query's arrows, Tab and Enter -- Enter on a query nothing matched too -- are spent until a frame that shows the list lands, and so are they after a refused or torn write, a frame of the other plane, a resize or a resume, until a frame shows the list again (`app_render_runtime.zig:1002-1003`, `:1022-1026`; `input_completion_runtime.zig:798-807`); every key the list takes, spent or not, asks for that frame (`app_input_runtime.zig:310-330`, `:1013-1020`, `:1317-1321`, `:1405-1410`). -=======
-| full-screen TUI | ui | partial | Opt-in and narrower than upstream's. `XFX_TUI=1` on a bare `xfx` takes the terminal into raw mode on its **normal** buffer: the band, the transcript and the composer live there for the life of the session and never move, and mouse reporting is never enabled, so the terminal's own scrollback and wheel keep working. The alternate screen is an owned excursion and nothing else -- `\x1b[?1049h` is written only to review a change too big for the band to show, for as long as that question is unanswered (below), and for the full transcript `C-o` opens, for as long as it is open (its own row below); a session that does neither, however long, writes it never. Under tmux the kitty keyboard push is omitted, because sending it there breaks key input. Raw mode is an obligation rather than a feature: the entry bits (`ECHO`/`ICANON`/`IEXTEN`/`ISIG` clear, `CS8`, `VMIN=1`, `VTIME=0`) are asserted positively while the child runs, and the `termios` captured before entry is put back on exit -- onto the descriptor it was captured from, and whether or not the screen could still be written. The band is a divider, the composer, and a hint row at the bottom of the screen -- with one more row above the divider while a turn is running, saying what it is doing, and a block of rows between the two while that turn is waiting to be told whether it may change something; every frame is one write wrapped in synchronized output (`?2026h`/`l`) -- a frame that also grows the document carries those rows inside the same update, ahead of the band, as upstream's frame carries its movement bytes (`composeWireFrame`, `src/ui/render_engine/terminal_diff.zig:604-645` at `c1db919`), so a terminal that honours the mode never presents a new row with the band still where the scroll left it -- and what that write carries is the **difference** between what the terminal is holding and what it should be holding: the band keeps a shadow grid of grapheme cells, wide-cell continuations and attribute state, builds the grid the frame would leave, and emits a `CUP`, replacement text and an `EL` for the rows that changed and for nothing else -- so a keystroke costs the cell it changed and an animation phase costs the digit it turned over. **A frame whose cells, window title and caret are all unchanged writes zero bytes**, which is what keeps an idle session silent between the loop's 8 ms ticks. The shadow is advanced only by a write that succeeded, so a refused frame is owed again rather than believed; a Ctrl-L and the resume after a `SIGTSTP` mark the screen externally damaged, and the frame after either opens with the Phase-1 erase -- the rows a shrinking band gave back, then `CUP` to the band's top row and `ED` -- because a forgotten shadow is *unknown* rather than blank, and a diff that treated it as blank would rewrite the band's own columns and leave whatever the shell put beside them on every band row. The erase starts at the band's top row and never above it, so the terminal's own document survives a resume. A resume also re-arms the window title: the restore that hands the terminal back pops the title stack, so the title the session set is gone, and the band owes a fresh `OSC 2` -- one push per time the terminal is taken and one pop per time it is given back, so a stop/resume cycle leaves the stack balanced. The optimization is judged by the screen it leaves rather than by the bytes it saves: the `fault-injection` build keeps the Phase-1 whole-band painter behind a compile-time seam (`Fault::FullPaintReference`, absent from every released binary), and `scripts/smoke-tui.sh`'s scenario 13 drives identical facts through both painters on a real pseudoterminal and requires the final cells, attributes and caret to be equal. **The terminal's window title is set** -- `OSC 2 ; xfx · <model>`, with every control stripped out of the model label so a configured name cannot close the sequence and have the rest of itself executed -- and it is borrowed rather than taken: the mode set pushes the terminal's own title onto its stack (`CSI 22 ; 2 t`) and every restore path, normal and abnormal, pops it back (`CSI 23 ; 2 t`). A title is written as its own `OSC 2` right ahead of the frame's synchronized update and never inside it, which is where upstream's lands: upstream writes a title the moment it changes, as a write of its own between frames (`syncTerminalTitleWith`, `src/core/app/app_session_runtime.zig:3608-3629`; `setTerminalTitleLabel`, `src/ui/render.zig:723-733` at `c1db919`), so its bytes reach the terminal after one frame's `?2026l` and before the next one's `?2026h`; the output check refuses an update led by anything else, or by a title other than the one the band owes. The line-oriented shell sets no title and pushes nothing. **What has already reached the terminal's own scrollback is never rewritten**: the diff writes only at or below the band's top row, plus the rows a shrinking band gave back, and a document append still scrolls the screen with real linefeeds -- committed rows stay the terminal's. A terminal smaller than 6 rows or 20 columns is refused with a reason rather than painted over. **What those rows say**: the divider spans the screen, the composer shows what has been typed into it behind its prompt marker, and the hint row says what a turn would be run with -- in upstream's order, joined by upstream's separator: a call to action when the configured provider has no usable credential (a Gateway with no bearer token or a llmux with no URL -- both refuse every turn, and the row says so before a prompt is spent on one), the permission mode, and a compact model label that drops the provider prefix and the `claude-` family prefix. A refusal takes the left of it whole when one has just happened, and `esc again to clear` sits flush with the last column while the double-Esc gesture is armed, the row's colours closed before its padding as upstream's are (`input_presentation.zig:551-567`). On a screen too narrow for all of it the segments are **dropped whole, from the right**, rather than clipped mid-number: the permission mode gives way in front of the model rather than the model behind it, and the right-hand warning is reserved before the left is measured, because it is about a key that is already armed. The meter upstream ends that row with is here too and is filled from a completed turn's own `input_tokens` over the context window the provider's catalog publishes, shown only when the session has both numbers and dropped whole rather than defaulted when it has one; what is still absent is the shortcut legend. **While a turn is in flight there is one row above the divider**, and only then: it says `• Thinking` with how long the turn has been running, or names the tool that is running instead of saying `Thinking` at all -- a turn that has gone quiet because a tool is taking a minute otherwise looks exactly like a turn that has gone quiet. The marker blinks every 500 ms, counted in the renderer's own 50 ms animation phases rather than on a second clock beside it, and the row asks for a frame when its text changes rather than on every phase, so a band whose row says the same thing is not repainted twenty times a second and an idle band asks for nothing at all. The row is the **band's** while it exists: it takes its row from the bottom of the document exactly as a composer that grew by one does, and the divider, the composer and the caret do not move when a turn starts. It is **exactly the turn's lifetime**: the runtime says when a turn begins and says when it ends, in order on the one channel that carries everything else it says, and the band draws the row between the two. Told rather than counted, and that is correctness rather than taste -- a prompt is accepted the moment it is typed and may then wait behind another turn for a minute (the hint row is where waiting is said, and a waiting prompt gets no row), and the place a finished turn holds is given back *after* its conclusion is sent, so a band counting places would announce a turn that had not started and start a clock for one that no longer exists. **The clock is one turn's**: a prompt queued behind a running one does not restart it, and when that turn ends the row begins again from zero for whatever turn the runtime starts next rather than carrying the finished one's elapsed time forward. A `/model` travels on the same channel but is not a turn, and no row appears for one. Two things named rather than implied: the elapsed clock **stops while xfx is waiting for a decision**, because that interval measures the person rather than the model -- the approval panel below is what pends one, and the row itself is off the band while a question is up, as upstream's is (`render_input.zig:705`), and comes back at the elapsed time it stopped at; and the token count upstream shows beside the elapsed time is **absent**, because the streamed event set carries no usage -- the row says nothing about tokens rather than reporting a zero nobody measured. What is *above* the divider is the turn, described below. The band's size is asked of **standard output**, because that is the screen it is drawn on; raw mode is entered on standard input, because that is where input arrives; and a redirected session whose two ends are different terminals gets no cursor report at all, so it starts at row 1 and pushes nothing. A screen that refuses every frame for half a second of wall-clock time ends the session with the first of those errors, on a terminal the exit still puts back, rather than being retried invisibly forever; the budget is measured on a clock rather than in repaints, because the 8 ms tick is the longest a turn waits and not the shortest. The exit clears from the band's top row downward (`app_lifecycle.zig:578-593`), and only when a band was really painted: a session that left before its first frame -- a Ctrl-D that arrived with the cursor report -- moves no cursor and erases nothing. At launch it asks the terminal where the cursor is (CSI `6n`, 100 ms deadline, row 1 if the terminal does not answer; the row under it when the cursor is past the first column) and pushes the shell output above that row into scrollback -- or, with `/settings`' `Startup scrollback` off, starts on that row under it and pushes only what leaves fewer than eleven document rows (`app_lifecycle.zig:944-988`) -- moving the cursor to the bottom row first, because a linefeed scrolls a terminal only from the bottom margin and from anywhere else merely walks the cursor down -- so the band never paints over what was already on screen; a keystroke typed while the query is in flight is deferred, not swallowed. Restoration is proven one case per exit path, each on a real pty: the normal exit, `SIGTERM`/`SIGHUP`/an external `SIGINT` (the child dies *by the signal*, which is what proves the handler reset the disposition and re-raised rather than fabricating a status) -- **except a `SIGINT` the process was started ignoring** (`nohup … &`), which stays ignored, so a `kill -INT` changes nothing and every command the session runs inherits the ignore, as upstream's TUI catches no `SIGINT` at all (`installAbnormalExitHandlers` owns `SIGTERM` and `SIGHUP` only, `app_lifecycle.zig:82-104` at `c1db9191`) -- and `SIGTSTP`/`SIGCONT` including a **second** `SIGTSTP` after a resume, which is what proves the stop handler was reinstalled. Every one of those is an ordinary transition rather than a race, because the owned signals are blocked across the whole of it -- from before the `tcsetattr` that takes the terminal until after the last handler is installed -- so a supervisor that signals xfx during startup finds a handler rather than a default disposition and a raw terminal. The signals named here are the ones a *supervisor or an operator* sends: with `ISIG` cleared a **typed** Ctrl-C or Ctrl-Z generates no signal at all, and arrives as a byte for the input decoder. Ctrl-C is a byte, not a signal, and it means what upstream's semantic Ctrl-C means (`app_input_runtime.zig:1675-1712` at `c1db919`): over a running turn with a draft in the composer it takes the draft and nothing else; otherwise every press is a press of one three-second exit window -- the first arms it, says `press ctrl+c again to exit` on the hint row for as long as it lasts (the `/help` catalog's row included), and stops the running turn or throws the idle draft away; a second inside it leaves with **130**, disjoint from the external `SIGINT` above, which still kills the process by the signal unless the launcher ignored it; any other keystroke closes the window early. A stopped turn leaves upstream's line, `▪ Cancelled · What can xfx do differently?`, at the keystroke: the answer stops where it was on the screen (the line it had not finished is not painted, nor is anything still crossing from the runtime) and no `✗ system:` or summary follows; a running command is killed at the keystroke with its process group, its row `└ Cancelled <command>` and its line `▪ Cancelled <command> · …` the turn's (the UI raises the turn's own flag, which the command's supervisor polls), where a running file call runs to its end first -- **and the interrupt drops whatever was queued behind it**, said as `⊘ queue: dropping what was queued behind it as well.`, because a queue that started running a moment after the user asked everything to stop would be the surprise the band's `┋` row is there to prevent; a prompt typed *after* the interrupt is a new intention and still runs, which the interrupt is told the boundary of rather than left to guess. What the model is told about the stopped turn is upstream's: the cut-off answer closed with `The previous response ended before completion.` (or the tools that finished, named), a call the turn stopped under -- one that never ran, a command the user stopped, or a question the user cancelled -- answered `aborted by user` as a failure, then the `<turn_aborted>` user message (`session.zig:52-64`, `:2695-2747`); any failed tool result is sent as a failure (`error-text` on the Gateway, `is_error` on llmux; `vercel_protocol.zig:742-747`), and a refusal as `execution-denied` with the refusal as its reason (`vercel_protocol.zig:742-744`; `is_error` on llmux, which has no word of its own). A typed Ctrl-Z still binds nothing -- upstream's suspends (`app_input_runtime.zig:862-866@481104fd`), so here only a `SIGTSTP` stops the session; owner unit: **CTRL-Z** (queued) -- and Ctrl-D is not an exit while a turn is running. Escape is a two-tap: over a running turn a second one within a second stops it, and the hint row says `esc again to interrupt` (narrower: `esc esc interrupt`, `esc esc`) in between; at an idle prompt with a draft a second one within 500 ms clears the composer, and the row says `esc again to clear`, because nothing else in this phase discards a draft without a key that says so; at an empty idle composer Escape arms nothing (`app_input_runtime.zig:3210-3213`). A prompt submitted while a turn is running is upstream's steer, delivered into that turn at upstream's boundaries (`worker_runtime.zig:1080-1104`, `orchestrator.zig:157-261`): over a tool-free answer at once -- the answer cut where it had got to and kept as the assistant's message -- while a tool runs after its results, and with the model's answer after it, the turn going on; the model is told each in upstream's `<user_steering>` wrapper and a later turn replays the words as typed. It is the band's `┋ <prompt>` row in the dim grey until the turn takes it and its card is written then; Up at an empty composer takes the newest back while it still waits for a tool (`popQueuedSteerForEdit`); a stop drops steers with the queue, and one the turn never took runs as the next prompt. A request the turn retries (a status the server says is transient, a connection that never opened) replaces the activity row with upstream's recovery label, `⚠ Provider unavailable · HTTP 500 · server_error: upstream exploded · retrying request in 1s`, counting down to the next attempt and kept through a retried answer arriving, then `✓ recovered · attempt N` for 1.5 s or until the turn ends (`worker_status.zig:5`, `:221-235`), a label too wide for the screen wrapping onto up to three rows under its mark (`render_input.zig:406`, `:689-697`); the retry budget is xfx's own three attempts, not upstream's (re-verified against fx v0.0.16, `481104fd`, which changes nothing a retry shows: upstream's label, `recoveryLabel`, `src/core/shared/types.zig:539-606@481104fd`, differs from 0.0.13's only by an action the retry path never takes, and its count still rounds up, `format_retry_label`, `src/core/output/worker_status.zig:201-219`, `:212@481104fd` -- xfx's `src/tui/retry.rs:369-381`, `:384-391`, with its three causes at `:252-261`; the statuses retried are still 429, 500, 502, 503 and 504, `fromHttpStatus`, `src/core/agent/stream_provider.zig:480-492`, and `isRetryableModelFailure`, `src/core/agent/runtime/orchestrator.zig:4496-4501@481104fd`, as xfx's `src/gateway/mod.rs:994-995`; 0.0.16's one change on that path, `fe7925b2`, deletes a stall stop a status failure could never reach; and upstream's turn still never ends on a transient failure, backing off 250 ms, 1 s, 2 s and on to 30 s and slowing to once a minute after fifteen minutes, `src/core/agent/runtime/model_response_recovery.zig:5-9`, `:270-282@481104fd`, where xfx's three attempts wait 250 and then 500 ms, `src/gateway/mod.rs:59`, `src/agent/machine.rs:78-85`, `:199-207`), and Escape over a retry arms the interrupt because upstream pauses only its unbudgeted wait for connectivity, which xfx does not have. That decoder is the session's own: bytes in, keystrokes out, one machine fed in arrival order (the keystrokes the launch probe read with the cursor report included), with a lone `ESC` resolved as the Escape key only after 50 ms of quiet, an unfinished `CSI` waited for however long the terminal takes, an unknown sequence bounded at 32 bytes and reported as a keystroke with no binding rather than as a phantom Escape, `ESC` followed by a control byte delivered as the Escape key *and* that byte -- except `ESC DEL`, which is Alt-Backspace and deletes back over one word of word characters, as upstream's does (`composer_deletion.zig:103-136`) where `C-w` deletes back to whitespace, and `ESC CR`/`ESC LF`, which is Alt-Enter and inserts a newline (`escape_parser.zig` at `c1db919`, :515-519), because replaying either would make two of them two Escapes inside the double-Esc window and throw the whole draft away, and an Alt-Enter a submit besides -- and **no** C0 byte or control scalar ever delivered as text -- a tab included, because a control that reached the composer would reach the terminal again on submit; a tab is a *named keystroke* instead, which the approval panel walks its choices with and the composer has no binding for. **Key reports are decoded in both of their spellings** -- kitty's `CSI <key> ; <mods> u` and `modifyOtherKeys`' `CSI 27 ; <mods> ; <key> ~` -- through one port of upstream's `kittyUnicodeKeyAction` (`escape_parser.zig` at `c1db919`, :185-266), in its branch order: a Ctrl letter is that letter's control byte and therefore its binding, Escape, Enter with a modifier, Alt-Backspace, Alt-b/f, Super+Z and the keypad are the keys they already are here, Caps Lock and Num Lock are not modifiers, and a key **release** is never a keystroke; Super+A, Super+C and Super+X are the composer's selection (:223-231) -- select the whole draft, copy it, cut it, through the clipboard `/copy` uses, the copy silent and the cut taking the text out only once the clipboard has it (`cutSelection`, `input_selection_runtime.zig:32-42`), the selected run painted in reverse video (`input_presentation.zig:995-1021`); Alt-d deletes the word to the right; Ctrl+O opens the full transcript; and a page key, bare or held with a modifier (`pageKeyAction`, :169-173), pages whichever surface has the keys -- the full transcript while it is open, the list of an open menu or picker by the composer's row cap (`routeComposerVertical`'s `picker_delta`, `input_completion_runtime.zig:222-233@481104fd`; `routeHelpMenuMove`, `:465-483`), and otherwise the composer's caret by the same number of rows (`fx-p5-input-row-limit-41x120-at-pgdn`, `-help-pgdn`, `-slash-pgdn`, `-grown-pgup`). Three of fx's lists that page so take no page key here yet: `/settings`' menu (`routeSettingsMenuMove`, `:431-463@481104fd`); the `/model ` column with its effort and speed stages (`navigateModelPicker`, `:1072-1096@481104fd`); and `/mcp `'s argument completions, which fx counts as a slash list (`slashCompletionCount` over the registry's `arguments`, `command_specs.zig:578-585`, `:876-889@481104fd`; those are `/mcp`'s verbs and servers, `main.zig:441-445`, `app_mcp_runtime.zig:459-468@481104fd`) and so pages by `picker_delta` (`input_completion_runtime.zig:255-260@481104fd`). xfx binds no page key on any of them: `SettingsAction::of` names none (`src/tui/settings.rs:539-551`), nor does the column's key set (`offer_to_column`'s `bound`, `src/tui/shell.rs`), nor `offer_to_mcp_args`. Owner: PAGE-KEYS-SETTINGS-COLUMN (the dispatcher's queue). `/mcp`'s menu takes a page key as one step, as fx's does (`routeMcpHostMenuMove`, `:424-429@481104fd`). What upstream resolves to a key xfx still does not have -- a selection-extending move, spelled on a letter (Ctrl+Shift+A/B/E/F, Alt+Shift+B/F) or with Shift on a page key, and the all-sessions toggle (`open_all_sessions`, :253) -- binds nothing beyond the plain move: Ctrl+Shift+A/B/E/F bind nothing, and Alt+Shift+B/F are the word moves with the Shift dropped, as the arrows' is (`escape_parser.zig:244`, `:248@481104fd`; a question's draft drops it in fx too, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:155-197@481104fd`), until the composer has a selection to extend (owner unit **COMPOSER-SELECTION-EXTEND**, queued); Shift-Tab, upstream's `toggle_permission_mode` (:254), is the permission-mode cycle (the `/permissions` row). A selection-extending **arrow** is the exception and is not a dead key: kitty's Up and Down (`57352`/`57353`) and the keypad's arrows, Home and End resolve through xfx's own cursor table, which drops Shift together with the selection it would have extended, so with Shift held -- alone or beside Ctrl or Alt -- the key makes the plain move it makes here unshifted (a word move for Left and Right under Ctrl or Alt) where upstream would extend a selection; the legacy `CSI 1 ; <mod> A` spelling takes the same table. **Super with an arrow is a one-character or one-row step here, where upstream jumps**: Super-Left and -Right to the line's start and end, Super-Up and -Down to the draft's (`modifiedArrowAction`, `escape_parser.zig:74-82@481104fd`; an approval's amendment the same, `src/ui/input/runtime.zig:82-83`); xfx's cursor table reads Super on Left and Right as the step (the decoder's `ComposerLeft`/`ComposerRight`, which only an approval's choice tells apart) and on Up and Down as the arrow (the decoder's `DraftStart`/`DraftEnd`, which only an approval tells apart: its amendment takes them to the draft's edges, as upstream's does). Owner unit: **COMPOSER-SUPER-ARROWS** (queued). Every key herdr 0.9.3 (libghostty-vt, kitty flag 1) was captured sending in that form decodes to its binding, and its `u`-form Ctrl-U and Ctrl-D are driven end to end on a real pty (`.prd/tui-phase3/receipts/2026-10-06-herdr-csi-u-keys.md`). The grammar is stricter than upstream's -- one canonical decimal spelling per field, no extra field -- and not looser anywhere. Bracketed paste is framed: `?2004h` is enabled, the bytes between `ESC[200~` and `ESC[201~` are content rather than keys -- so a newline inside a paste does not submit it, a `0x03` does not cancel a turn, and an escape sequence is not obeyed -- and anything over 1000 codepoints is shown in the composer as `[Pasted text #N, M lines]` (`1 line` for one) and expanded back into the prompt when it is sent -- the prompt's card, the turn's estimate and a steer's card are that expanded text, every line on rows of its own, as upstream's card is (`input_submit_runtime.zig:705-706`, `user_message_card.zig:348-383`, `orchestrator.zig:5482-5485`) -- expanded to the text the frame made of it, which is the pasted bytes filtered, decoded as UTF-8 with anything that is not UTF-8 replaced, and with CR and CRLF line breaks normalized to LF, rather than to the bytes the terminal sent. **A prompt's card is shown in the frame that clears the composer**, as fx v0.0.16's 70611a91 shows one (`submit` queues the card in the `route_bytes` that takes the draft, `src/tui/shell.rs` `send`/`echo`): whole, when it fits between the document's last row and fx's fixed content bottom, `rows - 4` (`footer_rows`, `src/main.zig:173@481104fd`; `p5-transcript-submit` wire, the first frame after Enter); otherwise only its newest rows -- as many as that room holds, at least one, and no more than the frame's band leaves under the row above the card and the blank row over the activity row -- at the band's top, and the card itself, whole, in the next frame, once that one has landed (`buildPendingCardProjection`, `PendingCardPaintContext`, `transcriptAreaBeforePendingTail`, `noteCommittedFrame`: `src/core/app/app_render_runtime.zig:168-277`, `:2230-2238`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:289-307@481104fd`; `Shell::preview_room`, `preview_card`, `preview_landed`). A three-line prompt on a full 40-row screen previews its last line, five lines their last three, a 60-line pasted block its last 32 rows under the welcome (`p5-transcript-submit-multi`, `p5-submit-preview-*` wires; `.prd/tui-phase5/receipts/submit-card-preview/`). The preview is the band's rows, not the document's, so none of it reaches scrollback, and every screen after it is the one the card was written onto before. The card's frame follows the preview's in the same tick, with nothing read and no resize taken between them, as fx writes the card in its next frame once its preview's has committed (`noteCommittedFrame`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:289-307@481104fd`; `Shell::take_preview_shown`). What stays open is a write that fails, as it does between fx's two writes: the card's frame refused or torn before its rows, or the preview's frame torn, and then a screen that shrinks before the retry -- the preview's rows the terminal moves into its history, or keeps above the band where they are the document's to xfx after a resize, stay there, and the card is still written once, whole and in order (`a_failed_write_and_a_shrink_before_the_retry_leave_only_what_the_shrink_took`). The room is measured against the last landed frame, as fx measures from its last committed one (`src/ui/transcript/runtime.zig:9929-9932@481104fd`; five lines and their Enter in one read preview what the one-row composer's frame left, `p5-submit-preview-oneread`). Checked against fx at 40x120, 30x100 and 24x80. Written whole at once, where fx would still preview (its pending path is shut only by an open stream, paced output, Ctrl-O or a turn start its worker will not hold, `installPendingSubmission`, `src/core/app/input_submit_runtime.zig:1123-1150@481104fd`), so xfx previews less than fx there, with no capture of fx's frame for them: a card under a line that does not end on a blank row (fx's mid-line endpoint, `pendingCardLeadingAdvanceRows`, `src/core/app/app_render_runtime.zig:209-218`); under a fork's `original session` line (`raw_gap`); under a tool group still open; one submitted while the document still owes rows, a reset's welcome among them; on a screen of 8 rows or fewer, whose running band leaves the preview no row (`room` 0, where fx may still paint one row at its band's bottom, `PendingCardPaintContext.init`, `:185-187`); a card whose preview the band loses the room for before the preview's frame (the slash menu opened by a key read with the Enter, a steer's banner): written whole that frame, the preview gone for good; a resize before the preview's frame (the preview is dropped); a card sent after a resize and before a band frame of the new size has landed, which leaves nothing to measure from (fx measures from its last committed frame, `runtime.zig:9929-9932`, then one of the old size); a question or Ctrl-O taking the other plane before the preview's frame: the card is paid whole before `1049h`, as the barrier pays every row the primary plane owes (fx paints no pending card while the alternate screen owns rendering, `:1264-1268`); and the exit. The activity row under it is xfx's own, as under every card (T7). A pasted prompt taller than the screen reaches scrollback whole, every line once and in order, as the session's first prompt too (433464dd, `p5-transcript-tallfirst`). A collapsed block is an **atomic entity**: it is a *span* of the draft rather than a name to be searched for, so exactly one run of the draft is the block and a second copy of its summary -- typed, or pasted back off the screen, before it or after it -- is only ever the words it looks like. Every motion steps over it whole (left, right, both word moves, and the two vertical moves, which land in front of it rather than inside it, as upstream's land on a unit's start), so the caret is never inside a summary; every delete that overlaps it at all takes the whole of it, so one backspace at its right edge, one forward delete at its left edge, a word delete or either kill removes the block rather than damaging its name. The span moves with the text around it as that text is edited. A number a block was given is **never** given again in one session -- a removed block's number is spent -- and a line recalled from history brings its blocks back under **fresh** numbers, allocated from the same checked counter a paste spends, which is what keeps two live blocks from answering to one name; at the end of that counter a paste is refused visibly and a recall hands the line back as words and says so, rather than wrapping or reusing a number. Removing a block gives its bytes back to the budget for good: what was pasted stops being sent, and typing the same words in again is typing rather than repairing. **A paste ends with its delivery, as fx v0.0.16's does.** A delivery is one continuously readable run of input: every read until a readiness check finds nothing more -- one read or many, since a read takes at most 128 bytes -- and across the 32-read cap of one turn, which carries it into the next turn unsettled (`event_loop.zig:94-119@481104fd`). Within one delivery, input that arrives behind the end marker is taken for the clipboard's rather than the user's, and it is **never read as keys**: it is counted and dropped, a second end marker or a new paste's start included. The paste it followed is **kept**, and upstream's `! input: Input that arrived right after the paste was ignored.` is said once it has been (`paste_framing.zig:58-68,102-107,159-165`, `input_paste_runtime.zig:168-198@481104fd`, 9e0eab41; c1db919 refused the whole paste instead). So clipboard text carrying an `ESC[201~` and a newline, delivered in one go, cannot submit the composer, answer an approval or take a question's choice. Every byte there is the clipboard's whatever its origin: a readiness check cannot tell a key a person pressed from a byte the terminal is still writing. **Past that, xfx diverges from fx, for security: the input stays quarantined until it has been quiet for 50 ms.** fx's delivery is the terminal's, not the clipboard's: a paste longer than the pty's input queue (1022 bytes on macOS) arrives as the terminal refills it, a check can find the queue empty in between, and fx settles the paste there and reads the rest of the clipboard as keys (`event_loop.zig:94-119@481104fd`). Measured against a real xfx at fx's boundary (the PASTE-GAP receipt, `measure.txt`): on a plain pty the paste's own newline submitted it in 2 of 3 runs at 64 KiB and at 1 MiB, and through tmux on a terminal that does not strip the marker in every run from 4 KiB up -- tmux ends a paste at its first embedded `ESC[201~` and forwards the rest as keys (`tty-keys.c:213-214,641-646@7f2a35ad`). And no terminal layer can be relied on to strip it: kitty, Alacritty, VTE, foot and Windows Terminal do; WezTerm strips once, so a nested marker survives; xterm, iTerm2 and tmux's `paste-buffer -S` let the user turn the filter off; Terminal.app cannot be checked (`terminals.md` there). So the paste is still settled where fx settles it -- the same `PasteEnd`, at the same check, so every capture is fx's -- and after it every byte is counted and dropped like the bytes behind the marker, until two checks 50 ms apart find nothing with nothing read between them: a fact about the queue, not the clock, so a loop that stalls between two reads of a paste still reads it as one. The warning is said at the first check after anything was dropped, and **while dropped input keeps arriving it is said again once a second**, in xfx's own words saying what holds the quarantine then (`Decoder::PASTE_REPEAT`, `Hold`): `!
